1	"""GitHub trajectory mining for coding session distillation.
2	
3	Mines PR diff chains and issue-commit chains from GitHub repositories,
4	producing trajectory dicts suitable for normalization and distillation.
5	Designed to run on an L4 VM with network access and a GITHUB_TOKEN.
6	"""
7	
8	from __future__ import annotations
9	
10	import logging
11	import math
12	import re
13	from typing import Any
14	
15	from model_training.github_client import GitHubClient
16	
17	logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
18	
19	__all__ = ["mine_pr_diff_chains", "mine_issue_commit_chains", "search_quality_prs"]
20	
21	_FIXES_RE = re.compile(
22	    r"(?:fix(?:es)?|close[sd]?|resolve[sd]?)\s+#(\d+)", re.IGNORECASE
23	)
24	
25	_DEFAULT_EXCLUDE_LABELS = frozenset(
26	    {
27	        "dependencies",
28	        "documentation",
29	        "docs",
30	        "chore",
31	        "ci",
32	        "bot",
33	    }
34	)
35	
36	
37	def search_quality_prs(
38	    repo: str,
39	    max_results: int = 100,
40	    github_token: str | None = None,
41	    min_review_comments: int = 1,
42	    min_commits: int = 2,
43	    exclude_labels: list[str] | None = None,
44	) -> list[int]:
45	    """Search for high-quality merged PRs using the GitHub Search API.
46	
47	    Pre-filters PRs by review approval, comment count, label exclusion,
48	    and minimum commit count to identify PRs with meaningful review
49	    trajectories suitable for distillation.
50	
51	    Args:
52	        repo: GitHub repository in "owner/repo" format.
53	        max_results: Maximum number of qualifying PR numbers to return.
54	        github_token: Personal access token for GitHub API authentication.
55	        min_review_comments: Minimum number of comments for search query.
56	        min_commits: Minimum number of commits a PR must have.
57	        exclude_labels: Labels to exclude. Defaults to common non-code labels.
58	
59	    Returns:
60	        List of qualifying PR numbers.
61	    """
62	    client = GitHubClient(token=github_token)
63	    labels_to_exclude = (
64	        frozenset(exclude_labels)
65	        if exclude_labels is not None
66	        else _DEFAULT_EXCLUDE_LABELS
67	    )
68	
69	    query = (
70	        f"repo:{repo} is:pr is:merged review:approved comments:>{min_review_comments}"
71	    )
72	    per_page = min(max_results, 100)
73	    pages_needed = math.ceil(max_results / 100)
74	
75	    all_items: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
76	    for page in range(1, pages_needed + 1):
77	        data = client.get(
78	            "/search/issues",
79	            params={
80	                "q": query,
81	                "sort": "updated",
82	                "order": "desc",
83	                "per_page": per_page,
84	                "page": page,
85	            },
86	        )
87	        items = data.get("items", [])
88	        all_items.extend(items)
89	        if len(items) < per_page:
90	            break
91	
92	    total = len(all_items)
93	
94	    # Label filter
95	    after_label: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
96	    for item in all_items:
97	        item_labels = {lbl["name"] for lbl in item.get("labels", [])}
98	        if not item_labels & labels_to_exclude:
99	            after_label.append(item)
100	
101	    # Commit count filter
102	    result: list[int] = []
103	    for item in after_label:
104	        pr_number = item["number"]
105	        detail = client.get(f"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{pr_number}")
106	        if detail.get("commits", 0) >= min_commits:
107	            result.append(pr_number)
108	        if len(result) >= max_results:
109	            break
110	
111	    logger.info(
112	        "Search found %d candidates, %d after label filter, %d after commit filter",
113	        total,
114	        len(after_label),
115	        len(result),
116	    )
117	    return result
118	
119	
120	def mine_pr_diff_chains(
121	    repo: str,
122	    max_prs: int = 100,
123	    github_token: str | None = None,
124	    pr_numbers: list[int] | None = None,
125	) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
126	    """Extract PR diff chains from a GitHub repository.
127	
128	    Each chain represents an iterative coding session: initial commit ->
129	    review comments -> revision commits. The resulting trajectory records
130	    capture the back-and-forth of code review as a multi-step improvement
131	    process, suitable for distillation.
132	
133	    Returns trajectory dicts with the following fields:
134	    - task_id: f"pr_{repo}_{pr_number}"
135	    - task_description: PR title concatenated with body text
136	    - steps: list of commit diffs and review comments in chronological order
137	    - outcome: "merged" or "closed" depending on PR final state
138	
139	    Args:
140	        repo: GitHub repository in "owner/repo" format.
141	        max_prs: Maximum number of PRs to process. Defaults to 100.
142	        github_token: Personal access token for GitHub API authentication.
143	        pr_numbers: Optional list of specific PR numbers to mine. When
144	            provided, skips the paginated PR list fetch and fetches each
145	            PR individually.
146	
147	    Returns:
148	        List of trajectory dicts representing PR diff chains.
149	    """
150	    client = GitHubClient(token=github_token)
151	
152	    if pr_numbers is not None:
153	        prs = [client.get(f"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{n}") for n in pr_numbers[:max_prs]]
154	    else:
155	        prs = client.get_paginated(
156	            f"/repos/{repo}/pulls",
157	            params={"state": "closed", "sort": "updated", "direction": "desc"},
158	            max_pages=math.ceil(max_prs / 100),
159	        )
160	        prs = prs[:max_prs]
161	
162	    if not prs:
163	        return []
164	
165	    trajectories: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
166	
167	    for pr in prs:
168	        pr_number = pr["number"]
169	        title = pr.get("title", "")
170	        body = pr.get("body", "") or ""
171	
172	        commits = client.get_paginated(
173	            f"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{pr_number}/commits",
174	            max_pages=5,
175	        )
176	        reviews = client.get_paginated(
177	            f"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{pr_number}/comments",
178	            max_pages=3,
179	        )
180	
181	        # Build timestamped steps for chronological interleaving
182	        timed_steps: list[tuple[str, dict[str, str]]] = []
183	
184	        for commit in commits:
185	            sha = commit["sha"]
186	            msg = commit["commit"]["message"]
187	            ts = commit["commit"].get("committer", {}).get("date", "")
188	            detail = client.get(f"/repos/{repo}/commits/{sha}")
189	            files = detail.get("files", [])
190	            patches = []
191	            for f in files:
192	                patch = f.get("patch", "")
193	                if patch:
194	                    patches.append(f"--- {f['filename']} ---\n{patch}")
195	            timed_steps.append(
196	                (
197	                    ts,
198	                    {
199	                        "type": "commit",
200	                        "description": msg,
201	                        "content": "\n".join(patches),
202	                    },
203	                )
204	            )
205	
206	        for comment in reviews:
207	            ts = comment.get("created_at", "")
208	            timed_steps.append(
209	                (
210	                    ts,
211	                    {
212	                        "type": "review",
213	                        "description": "Review comment",
214	                        "content": comment.get("body", ""),
215	                    },
216	                )
217	            )
218	
219	        # Sort by timestamp so commits and reviews interleave chronologically
220	        timed_steps.sort(key=lambda x: x[0])
221	        steps = [step for _, step in timed_steps]
222	
223	        outcome = "merged" if pr.get("merged_at") is not None else "closed"
224	
225	        trajectories.append(
226	            {
227	                "task_id": f"pr_{repo}_{pr_number}",
228	                "task_description": f"{title}\n\n{body}".strip(),
229	                "steps": steps,
230	                "outcome": outcome,
231	            }
232	        )
233	
234	    return trajectories
235	
236	
237	def mine_issue_commit_chains(
238	    repo: str,
239	    max_issues: int = 100,
240	    github_token: str | None = None,
241	) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
242	    """Link GitHub issues to their fixing commits via commit message references.
243	
244	    Scans commit messages for "fixes #N", "closes #N", or "resolves #N"
245	    patterns to identify which commits address which issues. Groups linked
246	    commits as trajectory steps for distillation.
247	
248	    Returns trajectory dicts with the following fields:
249	    - task_id: f"issue_{repo}_{issue_number}"
250	    - task_description: issue title concatenated with body text
251	    - steps: list of commits referencing this issue in chronological order
252	    - outcome: "closed" or "open" from the issue state
253	
254	    Args:
255	        repo: GitHub repository in "owner/repo" format.
256	        max_issues: Maximum number of issues to process. Defaults to 100.
257	        github_token: Personal access token for GitHub API authentication.
258	
259	    Returns:
260	        List of trajectory dicts representing issue-commit chains.
261	    """
262	    client = GitHubClient(token=github_token)
263	
264	    raw_issues = client.get_paginated(
265	        f"/repos/{repo}/issues",
266	        params={"state": "all", "sort": "updated", "direction": "desc"},
267	        max_pages=math.ceil(max_issues / 100),
268	    )
269	
270	    # Filter out pull requests (GitHub issues API includes PRs)
271	    issues = [i for i in raw_issues if not i.get("pull_request")]
272	    issues = issues[:max_issues]
273	
274	    issue_numbers = {i["number"] for i in issues}
275	    issue_map = {i["number"]: i for i in issues}
276	
277	    repo_commits = client.get_paginated(
278	        f"/repos/{repo}/commits",
279	        max_pages=10,
280	    )
281	
282	    # Group commits by referenced issue number
283	    linked: dict[int, list[dict[str, Any]]] = {}
284	    for commit in repo_commits:
285	        msg = commit["commit"]["message"]
286	        refs = _FIXES_RE.findall(msg)
287	        for ref in refs:
288	            issue_num = int(ref)
289	            if issue_num in issue_numbers:
290	                linked.setdefault(issue_num, []).append(commit)
291	
292	    trajectories: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
293	
294	    for issue_num, commits in linked.items():
295	        issue = issue_map[issue_num]
296	        title = issue.get("title", "")
297	        body = issue.get("body", "") or ""
298	
299	        steps: list[dict[str, str]] = []
300	        for commit in commits:
301	            sha = commit["sha"]
302	            msg = commit["commit"]["message"]
303	            detail = client.get(f"/repos/{repo}/commits/{sha}")
304	            files = detail.get("files", [])
305	            patches = []
306	            for f in files:
307	                patch = f.get("patch", "")
308	                if patch:
309	                    patches.append(f"--- {f['filename']} ---\n{patch}")
310	            steps.append(
311	                {
312	                    "type": "commit",
313	                    "description": msg,
314	                    "content": "\n".join(patches),
315	                }
316	            )
317	
318	        trajectories.append(
319	            {
320	                "task_id": f"issue_{repo}_{issue_num}",
321	                "task_description": f"{title}\n\n{body}".strip(),
322	                "steps": steps,
323	                "outcome": issue.get("state", "open"),
324	            }
325	        )
326	
327	    return trajectories
328

1	**LoRA Fine-Tuning Strategy**
2	
3	**for Rune's Qwen3.5-9B Adapter Pipeline**
4	
5	Elixir Trials --- March 31, 2026 (Rev. 2)
6	
7	1\. Pipeline Overview
8	
9	Rune's adapter pipeline is a single sequential process with two stages.
10	Stage 1 (QLoRA bootstrapping) produces a corpus of high-quality adapters
11	via gradient descent. Stage 2 (hypernetwork training) trains a model to
12	approximate Stage 1's output in real time. The hypernetwork is the
13	production system; QLoRA is the mechanism that creates its training
14	data.
15	
16	The core objective is to produce adapters that encode *procedural
17	knowledge* from coding trajectories---not just factual recall. An
18	adapter generated from a trajectory of "generate → execute → error →
19	diagnose → fix" should enable the model to apply that repair pattern to
20	novel, structurally similar errors. This is distinct from RAG (which
21	consumes context tokens) and from full fine-tuning (which risks
22	catastrophic forgetting).
23	
24	  --------------------- ------------------------------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------
25	                        **Stage 1: QLoRA Bootstrapping**                        **Stage 2: Hypernetwork Production**
26	  Purpose               Build high-quality adapter corpus                       Generate adapters in real time during agent loop
27	  Mechanism             Gradient descent on trajectory SFT data                 Single forward pass through DocToLoraHypernetwork
28	  Starting checkpoint   DeltaCoder adapter (warm-start)                         Sakana qwen\_4b\_d2l aggregator (transfer)
29	  Rank                  64 (inherited from DeltaCoder)                          8--32 (see Section 3.3)
30	  Latency               Minutes per adapter (GPU-bound)                         Milliseconds per adapter (CPU-capable)
31	  Quality               High (gradient-optimized)                               Approximate (amortized)
32	  Volume                50--100 adapters (bootstrapping)                        Unlimited (on-demand generation)
33	  When it runs          Offline corpus building + occasional high-value tasks   Real-time within the agent loop
34	  --------------------- ------------------------------------------------------- ---------------------------------------------------
35	
36	Once the hypernetwork is trained, QLoRA's ongoing role shrinks to
37	producing occasional adapters for tasks where the hypernetwork's
38	approximation is insufficient. Those adapters feed back into periodic
39	hypernetwork retraining.
40	
41	2\. Stage 1: QLoRA Fine-Tuning with DeltaCoder Warm-Start
42	
43	2.1 The DeltaCoder Warm-Start
44	
45	Rather than initializing new LoRA matrices from scratch, Stage 1 loads
46	**danielcherubini/Qwen3.5-DeltaCoder-9B** via
47	PeftModel.from\_pretrained, sets requires\_grad=True on the adapter
48	parameters, and continues training on Rune's trajectory data. This
49	provides three advantages:
50	
51	-   **GDN coverage inherited:** DeltaCoder already targets all 12 module
52	    types (5 GDN + 4 attention + 3 MLP). No manual module list
53	    configuration needed.
54	
55	-   **Convergence acceleration:** Starting from pretrained weights skips
56	    the initial convergence phase. DeltaCoder was trained on 50K
57	    CoderForge tool-call examples and DPO-aligned on self-correction
58	    pairs---directly relevant to Rune's trajectory domain.
59	
60	-   **DPO alignment preserved:** DeltaCoder's DPO stage on AceCode-V2
61	    preference pairs gives it self-correction behavior that maps to
62	    Rune's Phase 5 diagnose→repair loop.
63	
64	Prerequisite: Validate DeltaCoder before warm-starting. Load via
65	PeftModel.from\_pretrained, confirm no shape errors, run ≥50 HumanEval
66	samples. If it degrades the base model, fall back to fresh LoRA
67	initialization using DeltaCoder's target module list as the
68	configuration template.
69	
70	2.2 Training Hyperparameters
71	
72	  ------------------ ---------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
73	  **Parameter**      **Value**                                      **Rationale**
74	  LoRA rank          64 (inherited from DeltaCoder)                 Matching rank avoids weight\_head reshape; sufficient for code trajectories
75	  LoRA alpha         32 (alpha = rank/2)                            DeltaCoder convention; PEFT scale = 0.5 (conservative)
76	  Learning rate      2e-4                                           Constant schedule with warmup; cosine decay risks premature convergence on small datasets
77	  LR schedule        Constant + warmup (10 steps)                   Avoids oscillation on small trajectory datasets
78	  Loss computation   Assistant tokens only                          Mask system/user turns; compute loss only on model outputs
79	  Attention impl     eager (NOT flash\_attention\_2)                Flash + sample packing causes loss collapse on Qwen3.5
80	  Sequence length    4,096--65,536 tokens                           Depends on trajectory length distribution
81	  Precision          bf16                                           NF4 quantized base, bf16 adapter weights
82	  Batch size         16--32 effective (via gradient accumulation)   Per-device batch=1, grad\_accum=16--32
83	  Per-turn EOS       Yes                                            Train clean stop at turn boundaries
84	  Quantization       NF4 double-quantized                           Matches existing trainer.py; VRAM \~12--17GB
85	  Epochs             3                                              Standard for small trajectory datasets
86	  ------------------ ---------------------------------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
87	
88	2.3 GDN-Aware Target Modules
89	
90	The full target module list for Qwen3.5-9B, inherited from DeltaCoder:
91	
92	  ----------------------- ----------------------------------------------------------------- --------------------------------
93	  **Category**            **Modules**                                                       **Layers Affected**
94	  GDN projections         in\_proj\_qkv, in\_proj\_z, in\_proj\_b, in\_proj\_a, out\_proj   24/32 (indices where i%4 != 3)
95	  Attention projections   q\_proj, k\_proj, v\_proj, o\_proj                                8/32 (indices 3,7,11,\...,31)
96	  MLP projections         gate\_proj, up\_proj, down\_proj                                  All 32 layers
97	  ----------------------- ----------------------------------------------------------------- --------------------------------
98	
99	When warm-starting from DeltaCoder, this module list is already baked
100	into the adapter's config. When initializing fresh (fallback case), the
101	list must be set explicitly.
102	
103	2.4 Training Data Pipeline
104	
105	1.  **Collection:** The rune-agent loop (generate → execute → reflect)
106	    produces CodingSession trajectories stored as JSON via trajectory.py
107	
108	2.  **Formatting:** format\_for\_sft() converts trajectories to
109	    3-message chat format (system, user, assistant).
110	    format\_for\_distillation() handles the KL-divergence variant
111	
112	3.  **Augmentation:** augment\_trajectories() generates LLM-augmented
113	    variants via Ollama (3 variants per trajectory by default)
114	
115	4.  **Splitting:** split\_by\_task\_id() ensures train/test splits
116	    preserve task\_id integrity (no data leakage)
117	
118	Both successful and failed trajectories are training signals. Successful
119	trajectories encode working solution patterns. Exhausted trajectories
120	(max attempts reached) encode diagnose→repair patterns critical for
121	error recovery. The initial corpus should span at least 3 task types
122	(bug-fix, feature-impl, refactor).
123	
124	3\. Stage 2: Hypernetwork Training
125	
126	Once Stage 1 produces the adapter corpus, the hypernetwork is trained to
127	approximate QLoRA's output in a single forward pass. The hypernetwork is
128	the component that makes Rune's real-time adapter generation
129	possible---without it, every new trajectory would require minutes of
130	GPU-bound gradient descent before it could inform the model's behavior.
131	
132	3.1 Sakana Aggregator Transfer
133	
134	Training the hypernetwork from random initialization has been shown to
135	fail (farpluto/doc-to-lora-niah-qwen3.5-2B achieves 0% exact-match on
136	NIAH). The recommended path:
137	
138	1.  **Download** qwen\_4b\_d2l **checkpoint** from Sakana's
139	    SakanaAI/doc-to-lora repository. This variant is preferred over
140	    gemma\_demo or mistral\_7b\_d2l because its aggregator was trained
141	    on a Qwen-family model, making feature representations more
142	    transferable.
143	
144	2.  **Transfer aggregator weights** via transfer\_aggregator\_weights():
145	    loads aggregator.\* parameters, freezes them (requires\_grad=False).
146	
147	3.  **Retrain head.\* only** against the Stage 1 adapter corpus. This
148	    leverages Sakana's pretrained Perceiver feature representations
149	    while adapting the output projection to Qwen3.5-9B's architecture
150	    and Rune's trajectory domain.
151	
152	Note the distinction: DeltaCoder is the warm-start for *Stage 1* (the
153	QLoRA adapters). Sakana is the warm-start for *Stage 2* (the
154	hypernetwork itself). They operate on different components of the
155	system.
156	
157	3.2 KL-Divergence Context Distillation
158	
159	The training loop uses a two-pass strategy per step: (1) extract
160	activations from the base model given the trajectory in-context
161	(teacher), (2) generate adapter weights via the hypernetwork and apply
162	them to the base model with no trajectory in context (student), (3)
163	compute the blended loss:
164	
165	L = α · KL(student \|\| teacher) + (1 - α) · CE(student, labels)
166	
167	where α=0.5. The adapter must reconstruct the teacher's behavior from
168	weight-space alone---this is what makes it a *parametric* episodic
169	memory rather than a context-based one. An enhancement worth testing:
170	use base+DeltaCoder as the teacher instead of base alone, giving the
171	hypernetwork a stronger target distribution to approximate.
172	
173	3.3 Hypernetwork Rank: Why Rank-8, and When to Increase
174	
175	The current default rank of 8 comes from the Sakana qwen\_4b\_d2l
176	checkpoint, which was trained at that rank. Starting at rank-8 preserves
177	the transfer benefit. But rank-8 may be too conservative for
178	Qwen3.5-9B's 12-module GDN architecture, and the rank is the primary
179	knob controlling the expressiveness vs. cost tradeoff of
180	hypernetwork-generated adapters.
181	
182	The key constraint is the weight\_head output dimension, which scales
183	linearly with rank:
184	
185	  ----------------------- ----------------------------------- ----------------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
186	  **Hypernetwork Rank**   **weight\_head Output (approx.)**   **weight\_head Parameters**   **Practical Concern**
187	  8                       \~25M                               \~200M                        Fits current architecture; Sakana transfer applies directly
188	  16                      \~50M                               \~400M                        2× cost; likely the sweet spot for expressiveness vs. size
189	  32                      \~100M                              \~800M                        Approaching diminishing returns; consider chunked heads
190	  64                      \~200M                              \~1.6B                        weight\_head larger than some target models; not recommended for single linear projection
191	  ----------------------- ----------------------------------- ----------------------------- -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
192	
193	The Perceiver latent is 32 × 256 = 8,192 dimensions. Projecting from
194	8,192 to 200M (rank-64) via a single linear layer is a 1.6B-parameter
195	matrix---expensive to train and prone to overfitting on 50--100
196	trajectories. At rank-16 or above, the single weight\_head should be
197	replaced with a per-module or chunked head architecture. The
198	per\_rank\_gen flag in d2l\_config.py (currently set to False) provides
199	a starting point: it generates each rank dimension independently, which
200	distributes the projection across smaller matrices.
201	
202	**Recommendation:** Start at rank-8 to validate the pipeline end-to-end
203	with Sakana transfer. Once working, sweep rank ∈ {8, 16, 32} and measure
204	Pass\@1 vs. generation latency. If rank-16 closes \>80% of the gap to
205	QLoRA rank-64, adopt it. Enable per\_rank\_gen when rank exceeds 16.
206	
207	3.4 Training Parameters
208	
209	  ------------------- ------------------------------ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
210	  **Parameter**       **Value**                      **Source**
211	  Latent dimensions   32 latents × 256 dims          DocToLoraHypernetwork defaults
212	  Perceiver depth     4 layers, 8 heads              DocToLoraHypernetwork defaults
213	  Output rank         8 (initial; ablation target)   Sakana checkpoint + sweep plan
214	  Learning rate       2e-4                           D2LTrainConfig default
215	  KL blend α          0.5                            Equal weight to KL divergence and CE loss
216	  Temperature         2.0                            KL distillation temperature
217	  Gradient clipping   1.0                            D2LTrainConfig default
218	  Warmup steps        10                             D2LTrainConfig default
219	  Max input length    512 tokens                     Activation extraction window
220	  Scaling factor      0.16                           Bayesian-optimized (200 trials); Sakana default of 45.25× is far too aggressive
221	  ------------------- ------------------------------ ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------
222	
223	4\. Adapter Evolution and Lifecycle
224	
225	4.1 Fitness-Driven Selection
226	
227	Each adapter is scored using a weighted fitness function:
228	
229	φ(a) = 0.7 · pass\_rate(a) + 0.3 · generalization(a)
230	
231	where pass\_rate is measured on the training task and generalization is
232	measured on held-out tasks of the same type. The weight α=0.7 biases
233	toward specialization; it is an ablation target.
234	
235	4.2 Evolution Operations
236	
237	  --------------- ------------------------------------------------------------ ------------------------------------------------------------------------
238	  **Operation**   **Trigger**                                                  **Effect**
239	  Consolidate     ≥5 adapters for a task type, top-3 similarity \> threshold   TIES-merge top-3 into domain-level adapter
240	  Update          New adapter has higher fitness for same task type            Archive old adapter, register new one
241	  Forget          Adapter fitness \< 0.3                                       Soft delete (is\_archived = 1)
242	  Merge           Periodic sweep (evolution\_interval seconds)                 TIES/DARE merge top-3 per task type; new generation = max(parents) + 1
243	  --------------- ------------------------------------------------------------ ------------------------------------------------------------------------
244	
245	4.3 TIES vs DARE Merging
246	
247	**TIES** (density=0.5) prunes 50% of parameters and resolves sign
248	conflicts by majority vote---preferred when adapters are diverse
249	(consolidating across subtask types). **DARE** (drop\_rate=0.1) randomly
250	drops 10% and rescales---preferred when adapters are similar (averaging
251	multiple runs of the same task). Both are implemented in merging.py.
252	
253	4.4 Lineage Tracking
254	
255	The adapter registry stores parent\_ids (JSON list) and generation
256	(integer) for every adapter, enabling full lineage tracing. Write-once
257	enforcement ensures adapter weights and hashes are immutable---only
258	metadata (fitness, archived status) is mutable.
259	
260	5\. Validation and Kill-Switch Gate
261	
262	5.1 Primary Gate: Pass\@1 Improvement
263	
264	The kill-switch requires ≥5% Pass\@1 improvement on HumanEval over the
265	unmodified base model:
266	
267	-   **Benchmark:** HumanEval, 20--30 held-out tasks, k=5 samples per
268	    task
269	
270	-   **Formula:** Pass\@1 = (1/N) ∑ 1\[task\_i passes on ≥1 of k
271	    samples\]
272	
273	-   **Expected range:** 5--15% improvement signals real effect
274	
275	-   **If gate fails:** Stop and reassess before investing further GPU
276	    time
277	
278	5.2 Baselines
279	
280	  ------------------------------- ------------------------------------------ ---------------------------------
281	  **Condition**                   **Description**                            **Purpose**
282	  Vanilla model                   Qwen3.5-9B base (bfloat16, no adapter)     Lower bound
283	  RAG baseline                    Retrieved trajectory snippets in context   Context-space memory ceiling
284	  QLoRA (DeltaCoder warm-start)   Stage 1 adapter on same trajectory data    Weight-space upper bound
285	  Hypernetwork-generated          Stage 2 single forward pass                Production system quality check
286	  ------------------------------- ------------------------------------------ ---------------------------------
287	
288	5.3 Diagnostic Metrics (Non-Gating)
289	
290	-   **Adapter Frobenius norm \|\|**ΔW**\|\|\_F:** Must be meaningfully
291	    nonzero---confirms adapter modifies model behavior
292	
293	-   **Inter-adapter cosine diversity \> 0.1:** Confirms diversity, not
294	    mode collapse
295	
296	-   **Training loss convergence:** Should decrease without early plateau
297	
298	5.4 PBB-Inspired Procedural Encoding Test
299	
300	Beyond Pass\@1: generate an adapter from a trajectory containing a
301	specific algorithm, load it, and query with held-out inputs *not in the
302	trajectory and not in context*. If the adapter-loaded model executes the
303	procedure better than the base model on novel inputs, the adapter has
304	encoded procedural knowledge in weight space.
305	
306	6\. Inference Configuration
307	
308	6.1 Temperature Recalibration
309	
310	The current optimized temperature of 0.25 was derived on
311	Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-Instruct. DeltaCoder's model card documents 0.5 as the
312	hard minimum for multi-turn agentic use. After migration, re-run
313	scripts/optimization/run\_optimization.py (Bayesian optimization, 200+
314	trials) on the new stack.
315	
316	6.2 Sampling Defaults
317	
318	  -------------------- ----------------- ----------- ----------- --------------------------------------
319	  **Context**          **Temperature**   **Top-k**   **Top-p**   **Notes**
320	  Multi-turn agentic   0.6               20          0.95        DeltaCoder model card recommendation
321	  Deterministic/code   0.2--0.4          ---         ---         Lower for consistent output
322	  Exploration phases   0.7--0.8          30          0.95        Higher for diverse decomposition
323	  Repetition penalty   1.04              ---         ---         Bayesian-optimized on previous stack
324	  -------------------- ----------------- ----------- ----------- --------------------------------------
325	
326	6.3 Serving Architecture
327	
328	vLLM with S-LoRA unified paging enables serving with up to 8 concurrent
329	adapters. Single-GPU training coordinates via sleep/wake endpoints.
330	Multi-GPU uses pipeline parallelism (not tensor parallelism---vLLM issue
331	\#21471 documents LoRA corruption on PCIe without NVLink).
332

## Complete Data Format Pipeline Summary

I've thoroughly analyzed the data format pipeline for training in your Rune project. Here's the complete breakdown:

---

## **1. CodingSession Model**
**Location:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/shared/src/shared/rune_models.py`

A Pydantic BaseModel that tracks a complete agent coding session:

```python
class CodingSession(BaseModel):
    session_id: str
    task_description: str
    task_type: str
    adapter_refs: list[AdapterRef] = []  # References to LoRA adapters used
    attempt_count: int = 0
    outcome: Optional[str] = None  # 'success', 'exhausted', or None
```

**Related:** `AdapterRef` model with `adapter_id`, `task_type`, and optional `fitness_score`.

---

## **2. Trajectory Storage Structure**
**Location:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/trajectory.py`

Trajectories are persisted as JSON files (one per session) at `~/.rune/trajectories/{session_id}.json` (or via `RUNE_TRAJECTORY_DIR` env var).

### Record Structure (from `record_trajectory()`):
```python
{
    "session_id": str,
    "task_description": str,
    "task_type": str,
    "adapter_ids": list[str],
    "outcome": str | None,  # "success", "exhausted", or None
    "timestamp": str,  # ISO 8601 UTC
    "steps": [
        {
            "attempt": int,
            "generated_code": str,
            "stdout": str,
            "stderr": str,
            "exit_code": int,
            "tests_passed": bool,
            "description": str,  # Optional, used in distillation
            "canonical_solution": str,  # Optional, set on successful steps
        },
        # ... more steps
    ]
}
```

---

## **3. format_for_sft() - SFT Chat Format**
**Location:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/trajectory.py`

Converts successful trajectories to 3-message chat format for supervised fine-tuning.

**Inputs:**
- Trajectory dict with `outcome="success"` and at least one step with `tests_passed=True`

**Output:** List of message dicts:
```python
[
    {
        "role": "system",
        "content": "You are a Python code generator. Output only code, no explanation."
    },
    {
        "role": "user",
        "content": "<task_description>"
    },
    {
        "role": "assistant",
        "content": "<generated_code>"  # Code from the last successful step
    }
]
```

**Key behavior:**
- Returns empty list `[]` if `outcome != "success"` or no step has `tests_passed=True`
- Extracts code from the **last step where `tests_passed=True`**
- System prompt is a constant: `"You are a Python code generator. Output only code, no explanation."`

---

## **4. format_for_distillation() - KL-Divergence Distillation Format**
**Location:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py`

Converts successful trajectories to activation/teacher split records for KL-divergence context distillation.

**Inputs:**
- Trajectory dict with fields: `task_id` or `session_id`, `task_description`, `steps`, `outcome`

**Output:** List of record dicts:
```python
[
    {
        "task_id": str,  # For train/test splitting by task family
        "activation_text": str,  # Trajectory context + task description (NO answer)
        "teacher_text": str,  # activation_text + answer code
    },
    # One record per successful step
]
```

**Record generation logic:**
- Builds trajectory context from `step["description"]` fields (concatenated with newlines)
- For each step with `tests_passed=True`:
  - Creates `activation_base = trajectory_text + "\n" + task_description`
  - Extracts answer from `step["canonical_solution"]` or `step["generated_code"]`
  - Appends record with `teacher_text = activation_base + "\n" + answer`
- Returns empty list `[]` if `outcome != "success"` or no successful steps

---

## **5. augment_trajectories()**
**Location:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py`

Produces LLM-augmented variants using an Ollama model.

**Inputs:**
```python
trajectories: list[dict[str, Any]]  # Each has task_id, activation_text, teacher_text
n_variants: int = 3  # Up to 3 augmentation strategies
model: str = "qwen2.5-coder:1.5b"
ollama_base_url: str | None = None
```

**Output:** List of augmented records with same structure:
```python
[
    {
        "task_id": str,  # CRITICAL: inherited from source (prevents train/test leakage)
        "activation_text": str,  # LLM-paraphrased
        "teacher_text": str,  # LLM-paraphrased
    },
    # Up to n_variants records per input trajectory
]
```

**Augmentation strategies (in order):**
1. Paraphrase task description and trajectory
2. Reorder/drop steps while preserving logic
3. Rename variables throughout trajectory

---

## **6. split_by_task_id()**
**Location:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py`

Splits records at task-ID boundary with **zero task-ID crossing** between train and test.

**Inputs:**
```python
records: list[dict[str, Any]]  # Each has 'task_id' field
test_fraction: float = 0.2  # Fraction of unique task_ids for test
seed: int = 42
```

**Output:**
```python
tuple[list[dict[str, Any]], list[dict[str, Any]]]  # (train_records, test_records)
```

**Key behavior:**
- Groups records by unique `task_id`
- Randomly shuffles task IDs and splits at boundary
- Ensures **no task_id overlap** between train and test
- Augmented records with same `task_id` as source stay together
- Minimum 1 task_id goes to test set even if fraction rounds to 0

---

## **7. Additional Data Pipeline Functions**

### `generate_needle_dataset(n=20)`
Generates deterministic needle-in-haystack synthetic records for CI testing. Returns:
```python
[
    {
        "task_id": f"needle_{i}",
        "activation_text": str,  # Code context + query
        "teacher_text": str,  # activation_text + answer
    }
]
```

### `generate_trajectory_dataset(source="humaneval", max_tasks=None)`
Loads HumanEval tasks and converts to records:
```python
[
    {
        "task_id": str,  # From dataset, e.g., "HumanEval/0"
        "activation_text": str,  # prompt.rstrip() (no solution)
        "teacher_text": str,  # prompt + canonical_solution
    }
]
```

### `normalize_mined_trajectory(mined)`
Converts GitHub-mined trajectory from mining pipeline to distillation-ready format.

**Outcome mapping:**
- `pr_*` task_ids: `"merged"` → `"success"`, else `"failure"`
- `issue_*` task_ids: `"closed"` → `"success"`, else `"failure"`

**Step transformation:**
- Commit steps get `[Commit]` prefix, content as `generated_code`
- Review steps get `[Review]` prefix, empty `generated_code`
- Last commit step gets `tests_passed=True` and `canonical_solution` (only if success)

### `save_jsonl(records, path)` and `load_jsonl(path)`
Persist/load records to JSONL format (one JSON object per line).

---

## **Key Data Flow Diagram**

```
Coding Session (agent executing)
         ↓
record_trajectory() 
         ↓
Trajectory JSON file (~/.rune/trajectories/)
         ↓
     ┌───┴────────────────┐
     ↓                    ↓
format_for_sft()     format_for_distillation()
     ↓                    ↓
[system, user,       [task_id, activation_text,
 assistant]           teacher_text]
     ↓                    ↓
SFT Training         KL-Divergence Distillation
                          ↓
                   augment_trajectories()
                          ↓
                   [augmented records with
                    inherited task_id]
                          ↓
                   split_by_task_id()
                          ↓
                  [train], [test]
                  (zero task-id overlap)
```

---

## **Critical Design Notes**

1. **SFT Format** is simple: 3 messages (system, user, assistant) with fixed system prompt
2. **Distillation Format** separates activation (context) from teacher (context + answer) for KL training
3. **Task-ID Integrity**: Both augmented and original records inherit/preserve `task_id` to prevent training/test leakage
4. **Success Only**: Both `format_for_sft()` and `format_for_distillation()` return empty lists for non-successful trajectories
5. **Step Filtering**: Only steps with `tests_passed=True` contribute to training records
6. **Trajectory Context**: Built from step `description` fields (not generated code), making it suitable for LLM augmentation

---

## **Files Referenced**

1. `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/shared/src/shared/rune_models.py` - CodingSession & AdapterRef models
2. `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/trajectory.py` - SFT format functions
3. `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` - Distillation pipeline
4. `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/shared/src/shared/pipeline_config.py` - Config dataclasses
5. `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/tests/test_trajectory.py` - SFT format tests
6. `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py` - Distillation pipeline tests

## Summary: RTK Style Compressor for Diff Compression in Rune

Based on my research of the codebase, here's what I found:

### 1. **RTK Reference in Codebase**

The only explicit reference to "rtk style compressor" appears in a metadata prompt file found at:
- **Location**: `.entire/metadata/a0f7c6c4-406d-4876-a579-cf2bb752c7b9/prompt.txt`
- **Context**: Line 18 states: "We probably just need the diff of the final step, rather than each increment. We'll probably want to use an 'rtk' style compressor and to do away as much as possible with irrelevant parts of the diff."

This appears to be **design guidance** for a future feature, not yet implemented in the codebase.

### 2. **What RTK.md Reveals**

The `/Users/noahdolevelixir/.claude/RTK.md` document reveals:
- **RTK = Rust Token Killer** 
- It's a **token-optimized CLI proxy** for Claude Code (achieving 60-90% token savings on dev operations)
- It's NOT a diff compression tool, but rather a command execution wrapper that reduces token overhead
- There's a name collision warning: `reachingforthejack/rtk` (Rust Type Kit) is different

### 3. **Current State of Diff Handling in Rune**

The codebase currently processes diffs in `/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py`:

**In `mine_pr_diff_chains()` and `mine_issue_commit_chains()` functions (lines 188-194, 304-309):**
```python
patches = []
for f in files:
    patch = f.get("patch", "")
    if patch:
        patches.append(f"--- {f['filename']} ---\n{patch}")
```

**Current limitations:**
- Includes ALL file patches from GitHub commits without filtering
- No whitespace-only change detection
- No lockfile filtering (package-lock.json, poetry.lock, etc.)
- No auto-generated file filtering (node_modules, build artifacts, etc.)
- No token minimization for training data

### 4. **Diff Filtering Opportunities**

Based on the codebase structure, an "RTK style compressor" for diffs should:

**A. Filter irrelevant file types:**
- Lockfiles: `package-lock.json`, `poetry.lock`, `Cargo.lock`, `yarn.lock`, `uv.lock`, `go.sum`, `pom.xml`
- Node modules and dependencies: `node_modules/`, `__pycache__/`, `.venv/`, `.egg-info/`
- Build outputs: `dist/`, `build/`, `.pyc`, `*.o`, `.so`
- Generated files: `.pb.py`, `_pb2.py`, compiled protobufs
- IDE/tool configs: `.vscode/`, `.idea/`, `.DS_Store`

**B. Filter whitespace-only changes:**
- Lines with only formatting changes (spaces, tabs, blank lines)
- File reorganization without logic changes
- Line ending normalization

**C. Compress patch headers:**
- Remove full hunks for non-substantive changes
- Keep only the actual code logic changes

**D. Tokenization optimization:**
- Strip file paths to just the relative path
- Consolidate similar hunks
- Summarize large diffs as "multiple changes to {filename}"

### 5. **Where to Implement This**

The implementation location should be in:
- **File**: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_mining.py`
- **Functions**: `mine_pr_diff_chains()` and `mine_issue_commit_chains()`
- **Logic**: Add diff filtering/compression BEFORE line 194 and line 309 where patches are collected

Alternative/parallel location:
- Create a new utility module: `libs/model-training/src/model_training/diff_compressor.py`

### 6. **Design Implications**

From the training data strategy docs (methods.md):
- Training uses **trajectory records** with code execution context
- The final step's diff is what matters for distillation (format_for_distillation only uses the last successful step)
- **Token efficiency** is critical for training data pipeline (60-90% savings target per RTK philosophy)
- GitHub mining produces trajectory records with `steps[].content` containing raw patches

### Key Takeaway

**"RTK style compressor" means: minimize tokens in diffs by removing irrelevant content (lockfiles, whitespace-only changes, auto-generated files, etc.) while preserving the essential semantic code changes.** This aligns with the RTK tool's philosophy of "token-optimized" operations for Claude training infrastructure.

## What I'm designing for

**Goal:** Train the Sakana hypernetwork to generate step-level LoRA adapters that encode "where we are, where we want to go, what we tried" — proving that weight-space episodic memory beats context-window approaches.

**Data flow:** Mine GitHub PRs → extract per-step training pairs → QLoRA fine-tune (DeltaCoder warm-start) → adapter corpus → hypernetwork training

**Each training pair = one step:**
- **Input (activation):** PR description + current diff state + review feedback
- **Target (teacher):** The revision diff that correctly addresses the feedback
- **Compressed** via RTK-style filtering (strip lockfiles, whitespace, generated code)

**Negative examples:** DPO pairs — merged PRs preferred, closed-unmerged rejected.

---

## 1. Sources — Tiered Mining Strategy

**Phase 1: Bootstrap (10 repos, ~500 PRs, ~1500 step-level pairs)**

| Language | Repos | Why these |
|---|---|---|
| Python (4) | transformers, scikit-learn, fastapi, airflow | Rich review culture, diverse domains (ML, algorithms, web, orchestration) |
| TypeScript (2) | microsoft/TypeScript, vercel/next.js | Exceptional type-system reviews + high-velocity web |
| Rust (2) | denoland/deno, rust-lang/rust-analyzer | Systems + IDE compiler, clean MIT |
| Go (2) | pingcap/tidb, kubernetes/kubernetes | Bot-enforced issue linkage + richest review culture on GitHub |

50 PRs per repo via `search_quality_prs()` (merged, approved, 2+ commits, review comments, no bot/docs/deps labels).

**Phase 2: Expand (+15 repos, +750 PRs) — only after Stage 1 validation**

Add: llvm/llvm-project, duckdb, godot (C++); dotnet/roslyn, dotnet/runtime (C#); square/okhttp (Kotlin); flutter/flutter (Dart); scala/scala3 (Scala); OpenZeppelin (Solidity); pandas, numpy, pytest, langchain (more Python); tikv, swc-project/swc (more Rust); etcd, prometheus (more Go).

**Phase 3: Conditional** — only if evaluation shows domain gaps. Niche repos: tigerbeetle (Zig), trivy (security), cilium (eBPF).

## 2. Dataset Structure

Each PR produces N training pairs (one per review→revision cycle, plus the initial commit):

**Step 0 (initial implementation):**
```
activation_text: "## Task\n{pr_description}\n\n## Linked Issue\n{issue_body_if_available}"
teacher_text:    "{activation_text}\n\n## Solution\n{compressed_initial_diff}"
task_id:         "pr_{repo}_{pr_number}_step_0"
```

**Step N (review→revision):**
```
activation_text: "## Task\n{pr_description}\n\n## Current State\n{compressed_diff_at_step_N}\n\n## Feedback\n{review_comment}"
teacher_text:    "{activation_text}\n\n## Revision\n{compressed_revision_diff}"  
task_id:         "pr_{repo}_{pr_number}_step_{N}"
```

**DPO negative (closed-unmerged PR):**
Same structure, but `outcome: "rejected"`. Paired with merged examples from the same repo for contrastive training.

Metadata stored alongside but not in the activation/teacher text:
```json
{"repo": "...", "pr_number": 123, "step_index": 2, "language": "python", "task_type": "bugfix", "outcome": "merged"}
```

This format is directly compatible with the existing `format_for_distillation()` output schema — same three core fields (task_id, activation_text, teacher_text). `split_by_task_id()` and `augment_trajectories()` work without changes.

## 3. Pipeline Changes (minimal)

**New file:** `libs/model-training/src/model_training/diff_compressor.py`
- Filter: lockfiles, generated code, binary, configs, whitespace-only hunks
- Strip: redundant git headers, keep `--- filename ---` + semantic hunks
- Truncate: cap individual file diffs at configurable token limit, summarize overflow

**Modify:** `d2l_mining.py`
- Apply compression in `mine_pr_diff_chains()` when building patches
- No structural changes — compression is a filter on existing patch extraction

**New function in:** `d2l_data.py`
- `normalize_mined_pairs(trajectory) → list[record]` — extends existing `normalize_mined_trajectory()` to produce per-step pairs instead of one record per trajectory
- Reuses the existing record schema `{task_id, activation_text, teacher_text}`

**Modify:** `mine_github.py` (CLI)
- Add batch mode: accept a repos config (YAML/JSON) with per-repo limits
- Output: JSONL to local disk or S3 path

That's 1 new file, 1 new function, 2 modifications. Existing `augment_trajectories()`, `split_by_task_id()`, `save_jsonl()` all work unchanged.

## 4. Training Plan

**Phase A: Mining (CPU, ~1 week)**
1. Configure Phase 1 repos (10 repos × 50 PRs)
2. Run `search_quality_prs()` per repo → ~500 PR numbers
3. Mine with `mine_pr_diff_chains()` → ~500 trajectories
4. Compress diffs, normalize to pairs → ~1500 step-level records
5. Mine ~50 closed-unmerged PRs for DPO negatives
6. `augment_trajectories()` → 3x augmentation → ~4500 training + ~150 DPO
7. `split_by_task_id()` → 80/20 train/test
8. `save_jsonl()` → upload to S3

**Phase B: Stage 1 — QLoRA Corpus (L4, ~3-5 days)**
Per the report: DeltaCoder warm-start, rank-64, NF4 quantized, bf16 adapters.
- Fine-tune on mined pairs → one adapter per step-level trajectory
- Initial target: 100-200 adapters from the best pairs
- Kill-switch: ≥5% Pass@1 improvement on HumanEval

**Phase C: Stage 2 — Hypernetwork (L4, ~2-4 days)**
Per the report: Sakana aggregator transfer, freeze aggregator, train head.
- Training data: (activation_text, adapter_weights) pairs from Stage 1
- KL-divergence distillation: adapter-loaded model (student) vs. trajectory-in-context model (teacher)
- Rank sweep: {8, 16, 32}

**Phase D: Evaluate and Expand**
- If hypernetwork closes >50% of QLoRA gap → expand to Phase 2 repos
- If not → increase corpus size, enable per_rank_gen, or increase rank before expanding repos

## Complete Mining Pipeline Code Analysis

I've thoroughly explored the mining pipeline. Here's the complete breakdown:

### **1. CLI Script: `mine_github.py` (/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/scripts/mine_github.py)**

**Command-line Arguments:**
```
--repo OWNER/REPO (required)     - GitHub repository identifier
--mode {prs|issues|both}          - Mining mode (default: both)
--max N                           - Max PRs/issues to process (default: 100)
--token TOKEN                     - GitHub token (falls back to GITHUB_TOKEN env var, required)
-o, --output FILE                 - Output JSON file path (required)
--normalize                       - Apply normalize_mined_trajectory before output
--quality                         - Pre-filter PRs via Search API for quality
--min-reviews N                   - Min review comments for quality filter (default: 1)
--min-commits N                   - Min commits for quality filter (default: 2)
--exclude-labels L1,L2,...        - Comma-separated labels to exclude
```

**Flow:**
1. Parse args, resolve GitHub token (must be set)
2. Call `search_quality_prs()` if `--quality` flag set
3. Call `mine_pr_diff_chains()` if mode includes prs
4. Call `mine_issue_commit_chains()` if mode includes issues
5. Optional: apply `normalize_mined_trajectory()` to each trajectory if `--normalize` set
6. Write merged results to JSON output file

---

### **2. GitHub API Client: `github_client.py`**

**Class: `GitHubClient(token=None, base_url="https://api.github.com")`**

**Key Methods:**
- `get(path, params=None, max_retries=3) -> Any`: Single API endpoint GET with rate-limit retry
- `get_paginated(path, params=None, max_pages=10, per_page=100) -> list[Any]`: Paginated list endpoint, follows Link rel=next headers
- `_get_response(url, params=None, max_retries=3) -> httpx.Response`: Internal HTTP handler with 403 rate-limit retry (backs off and retries)

**Features:**
- Automatic auth via Bearer token header
- Automatic rate-limit retry with exponential backoff (uses Retry-After header)
- GitHub API v2022-11-28

---

### **3. Mining Module: `d2l_mining.py`**

**Public Functions:**

#### `search_quality_prs(repo, max_results=100, github_token=None, min_review_comments=1, min_commits=2, exclude_labels=None) -> list[int]`
- Uses GitHub Search API with query: `repo:{repo} is:pr is:merged review:approved comments:>{min_review_comments}`
- Default exclude labels: `{"dependencies", "documentation", "docs", "chore", "ci", "bot"}`
- Returns PR numbers that pass filters

#### `mine_pr_diff_chains(repo, max_prs=100, github_token=None, pr_numbers=None) -> list[dict]`
- Fetches closed PRs (merged or unmerged)
- For each PR: fetches commits and review comments
- Creates chronological interleaving of commits and reviews by timestamp
- Returns trajectory dicts with fields:
  - `task_id`: `f"pr_{repo}_{pr_number}"`
  - `task_description`: PR title + body
  - `steps`: list of `{"type": "commit"/"review", "description", "content"}` 
  - `outcome`: `"merged"` or `"closed"`

#### `mine_issue_commit_chains(repo, max_issues=100, github_token=None) -> list[dict]`
- Fetches all issues (filtered to exclude PRs)
- Scans repo commits for patterns: `fixes #N`, `closes #N`, `resolves #N` (regex: `_FIXES_RE`)
- Groups commits by referenced issue number
- Returns trajectory dicts with fields:
  - `task_id`: `f"issue_{repo}_{issue_number}"`
  - `task_description`: Issue title + body
  - `steps`: list of commits referencing the issue
  - `outcome`: `"closed"` or `"open"` from issue state

---

### **4. Data Pipeline: `d2l_data.py`**

#### **`normalize_mined_trajectory(mined: dict) -> dict`** 
**EXACT IMPLEMENTATION:**

```python
def normalize_mined_trajectory(mined: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Convert GitHub-mined trajectory to distillation-ready format."""
    task_id: str = mined.get("task_id", "")
    raw_outcome: str = mined.get("outcome", "")
    raw_steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = mined.get("steps", [])

    # --- Determine normalized outcome ---
    if task_id.startswith("pr_"):
        normalized_outcome = "success" if raw_outcome == "merged" else "failure"
    elif task_id.startswith("issue_"):
        normalized_outcome = "success" if raw_outcome == "closed" else "failure"
    else:
        normalized_outcome = (
            "success" if raw_outcome in ("merged", "closed") else "failure"
        )

    # --- Identify commit steps to find the last one ---
    commit_steps = [s for s in raw_steps if s.get("type") == "commit"]

    # --- Normalize steps ---
    normalized_steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    for step in raw_steps:
        step_type = step.get("type", "")
        step_description = step.get("description", "")
        step_content = step.get("content", "")

        if step_type == "commit":
            is_last_commit = commit_steps and step is commit_steps[-1]
            tests_passed = is_last_commit and normalized_outcome == "success"
            entry: dict[str, Any] = {
                "description": f"[Commit] {step_description}",
                "generated_code": step_content,
                "tests_passed": tests_passed,
            }
            if is_last_commit and normalized_outcome == "success":
                entry["canonical_solution"] = step_content
            normalized_steps.append(entry)
        elif step_type == "review":
            normalized_steps.append(
                {
                    "description": f"[Review] {step_content}",
                    "generated_code": "",
                    "tests_passed": False,
                }
            )

    return {
        "task_id": task_id,
        "session_id": task_id,
        "task_description": mined.get("task_description", ""),
        "steps": normalized_steps,
        "outcome": normalized_outcome,
    }
```

**Key Logic:**
- PR with `outcome="merged"` → `"success"`; otherwise `"failure"`
- Issue with `outcome="closed"` → `"success"`; otherwise `"failure"`
- **Last commit step only** gets `tests_passed=True` AND `canonical_solution` (when outcome is success)
- All other steps: `tests_passed=False`
- Review steps: description prefixed `[Review]`, empty `generated_code`
- Commit steps: description prefixed `[Commit]`, `generated_code=step_content`

#### **`format_for_distillation(trajectory: dict) -> list[dict]`**
**EXACT IMPLEMENTATION:**

```python
def format_for_distillation(trajectory: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
    """Convert trajectory to distillation records (activation/teacher split)."""
    if trajectory.get("outcome") != "success":
        return []

    steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = trajectory.get("steps", [])
    task_description: str = trajectory.get("task_description", "")
    task_id: str = trajectory.get("task_id") or trajectory.get("session_id", "")

    # Build trajectory context from step descriptions (no answer tokens)
    trajectory_parts: list[str] = []
    for step in steps:
        desc = step.get("description", "")
        if desc:
            trajectory_parts.append(desc)

    trajectory_text = "\n".join(trajectory_parts)

    # Build activation_text (context only, no answer)
    activation_base = f"{trajectory_text}\n{task_description}".strip()

    records: list[dict[str, str]] = []
    for step in steps:
        if not step.get("tests_passed"):
            continue

        # Extract answer from canonical_solution or generated_code
        answer: str = step.get("canonical_solution") or step.get("generated_code", "")
        if not answer:
            continue

        records.append(
            {
                "task_id": task_id,
                "activation_text": activation_base,
                "teacher_text": f"{activation_base}\n{answer}",
            }
        )

    return records
```

**Key Logic:**
- Only processes `outcome="success"` trajectories
- `activation_text` = step descriptions + task description (no solutions)
- `teacher_text` = activation_text + the answer (canonical_solution or generated_code)
- Only creates records for steps with `tests_passed=True`
- Each such step produces one distillation record with same `activation_text`, different `teacher_text`

#### **`augment_trajectories(trajectories, n_variants=3, model="qwen2.5-coder:1.5b", ollama_base_url=None) -> list[dict]`**
- Takes activation_text and teacher_text from trajectories
- Generates up to 3 LLM-augmented variants per trajectory using Ollama:
  1. Paraphrase task description
  2. Reorder/drop steps in trajectory
  3. Rename variables
- **CRITICAL:** Augmented records inherit source `task_id` to preserve split integrity
- Returns list of augmented records with same structure

#### **`split_by_task_id(records, test_fraction=0.2, seed=42) -> tuple[list, list]`**
- Splits at **task_id boundary** (not record boundary)
- No task_id can cross train/test boundary
- All records with same task_id stay together
- Minimum 1 task_id to test set
- Returns `(train_records, test_records)`

#### **`save_jsonl(records, path)` and `load_jsonl(path) -> list[dict]`**
- Simple JSONL persistence: one dict per line
- Creates parent directories if needed
- Filters empty lines on load

#### **`generate_needle_dataset(n=20) -> list[dict]`**
- Deterministic needle-in-haystack records (no LLM, suitable for CI smoke testing)
- 12 code templates + 12 slot value sets cycling through them
- Returns records with `activation_text`, `teacher_text`, `task_id` fields

#### **`generate_trajectory_dataset(source="humaneval", max_tasks=None) -> list[dict]`**
- Loads HumanEval dataset from Hugging Face
- Creates records with `task_id`, `activation_text` (prompt), `teacher_text` (prompt + canonical_solution)

---

### **5. Trajectory Module: `trajectory.py`**

#### **`record_trajectory(session_id, steps, outcome=None, task_description="", task_type="", adapter_ids=None) -> dict`**
- Persists trajectory to disk at `$RUNE_TRAJECTORY_DIR/{session_id}.json` (default: `~/.rune/trajectories/`)
- Returns `{"session_id", "file_path"}`

#### **`load_trajectory(trajectory_id: str) -> dict`**
- Loads from `$RUNE_TRAJECTORY_DIR/{trajectory_id}.json`
- Raises `FileNotFoundError` if not found

#### **`format_for_sft(trajectory: dict) -> list[dict]`**
**EXACT IMPLEMENTATION:**
```python
def format_for_sft(trajectory: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, str]]:
    """Convert trajectory into SFT-compatible chat format."""
    if trajectory.get("outcome") != "success":
        return []

    steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = trajectory.get("steps", [])
    successful_step = next(
        (s for s in reversed(steps) if s.get("tests_passed")),
        None,
    )

    if successful_step is None:
        return []

    task_description: str = trajectory.get("task_description", "")
    generated_code: str = successful_step.get("generated_code", "")

    return [
        {"role": "system", "content": SYSTEM_PROMPT},
        {"role": "user", "content": task_description},
        {"role": "assistant", "content": generated_code},
    ]
```

**Key Logic:**
- Only processes `outcome="success"` trajectories
- Finds **last step with `tests_passed=True`** (reversed search)
- Returns 3-message list: `[system, user, assistant]`
- System prompt: `"You are a Python code generator. Output only code, no explanation."`

```python
def save_jsonl(records: list[dict[str, Any]], path: str | Path) -> None:
    out = Path(path)
    out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with out.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for record in records:
            f.write(json.dumps(record, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
```
