- `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py` -- add pair extraction tests
- `scripts/mine_github.py:30-101,103-191` -- add batch mining mode

**Existing code reused unchanged:**
- `d2l_mining.py`: `mine_pr_diff_chains()`, `search_quality_prs()` -- mining core
- `d2l_data.py`: `augment_trajectories()`, `split_by_task_id()`, `save_jsonl()`, `load_jsonl()` -- downstream pipeline
- `github_client.py`: `GitHubClient` -- HTTP client with rate-limit retry

## Repo Selection Strategy

**Phase 1 (Bootstrap): 10 repos, ~500 PRs, ~1500 step-level pairs**

| Language | Repos | Rationale |
|---|---|---|
| Python (4) | huggingface/transformers, scikit-learn/scikit-learn, fastapi/fastapi, apache/airflow | Rich review culture, diverse domains (ML, algorithms, web, orchestration) |
| TypeScript (2) | microsoft/TypeScript, vercel/next.js | Exceptional type-system reviews, high-velocity web |
| Rust (2) | denoland/deno, rust-lang/rust-analyzer | Systems + IDE compiler, clean MIT |
| Go (2) | pingcap/tidb, kubernetes/kubernetes | Bot-enforced issue linkage, richest review culture |

50 quality-filtered PRs per repo via `search_quality_prs()`.

**Phase 2 (Expand, after Stage 1 validation):** +15 repos adding C++ (llvm, duckdb, godot), C# (roslyn, runtime), Kotlin (okhttp), Dart (flutter), Scala (scala3), Solidity (OpenZeppelin), plus more Python/Rust/Go.

**Phase 3 (Conditional):** Niche repos only if evaluation shows domain gaps.

```python
# libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_diff.py
"""RTK-style diff compression for training data preparation.

Filters irrelevant files (lockfiles, generated code, binary assets,
build artifacts) and truncates large diffs to minimize token overhead
in hypernetwork training pairs.

Operates on the concatenated diff format produced by mine_pr_diff_chains:
    --- src/main.py ---
    +real code
    --- package-lock.json ---
    +lockfile noise
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from pathlib import PurePosixPath

__all__ = ["compress_diff"]

_SKIP_FILENAMES: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    {
        "package-lock.json",
        "yarn.lock",
        "pnpm-lock.yaml",
        "poetry.lock",
        "uv.lock",
        "Pipfile.lock",
        "Cargo.lock",
        "go.sum",
        "Gemfile.lock",
        "composer.lock",
        "flake.lock",
        ".DS_Store",
        ".gitattributes",
        ".editorconfig",
    }
)

_SKIP_EXTENSIONS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(
    {
        ".lock",
        ".png",
        ".jpg",
        ".jpeg",
        ".gif",
        ".svg",
        ".ico",
        ".woff",
        ".woff2",
        ".ttf",
        ".eot",
        ".mp3",
        ".mp4",
        ".zip",
        ".tar",
        ".gz",
        ".pdf",
        ".pyc",
        ".pyo",
        ".so",
        ".dylib",
        ".dll",
    }
)

_SKIP_SUFFIXES: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "_pb2.py",
    "_pb2_grpc.py",
    ".pb.go",
    ".pb.h",
    ".pb.cc",
    ".generated.ts",
    ".generated.js",
    ".g.dart",
    ".min.js",
    ".min.css",
    ".map",
)

_SKIP_PATH_SEGMENTS: tuple[str, ...] = (
    "node_modules/",
    "__pycache__/",
    ".venv/",
    "vendor/",
    "dist/",
    "build/",
    ".next/",
    "coverage/",
    ".idea/",
    ".vscode/",
)

_SECTION_RE = re.compile(r"^(--- .+ ---)$", re.MULTILINE)


def _should_skip(filename: str) -> bool:
    """Check if a file should be filtered from training data."""
    path = PurePosixPath(filename)
    if path.name in _SKIP_FILENAMES:
        return True
    if path.suffix in _SKIP_EXTENSIONS:
        return True
    if any(filename.endswith(s) for s in _SKIP_SUFFIXES):
        return True
    return any(seg in filename for seg in _SKIP_PATH_SEGMENTS)


def _truncate(text: str, max_lines: int) -> str:
    """Truncate text to max_lines, appending a marker if truncated."""
    lines = text.split("\n")
    if len(lines) <= max_lines:
        return text
    kept = lines[:max_lines]
    kept.append(f"... ({len(lines) - max_lines} lines truncated)")
    return "\n".join(kept)


def compress_diff(content: str, max_lines: int = 500) -> str:
    """Filter irrelevant files and truncate large diffs.

    Parses the ``--- filename ---`` section format produced by
    ``mine_pr_diff_chains`` and removes lockfiles, generated code,
    binary files, and build artifacts.

    Args:
        content: Concatenated diff content with section headers.
        max_lines: Maximum total output lines.

    Returns:
        Filtered and truncated diff string.
    """
    if not content:
        return content

    parts = _SECTION_RE.split(content)

    # No section headers found -- fall through to truncation only
    if len(parts) == 1:
        return _truncate(content, max_lines)

    # parts = [preamble, header1, body1, header2, body2, ...]
    # re.split with a capturing group interleaves captured groups
    kept: list[str] = []
    idx = 1  # skip preamble (parts[0])
    while idx < len(parts):
        header = parts[idx]
        body = parts[idx + 1] if idx + 1 < len(parts) else ""
        # Extract filename from "--- filename ---"
        filename = header[4:-4]  # strip "--- " and " ---"
        if not _should_skip(filename):
            kept.append(header + body)
        idx += 2

    result = "".join(kept).strip()

    if not result:
        return ""

    return _truncate(result, max_lines)
```

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helpers for normalize_mined_pairs tests

def _make_mined_trajectory(
    task_id: str = "pr_owner/repo_42",
    task_description: str = "Add widget support",
    steps: list[dict[str, str]] | None = None,
    outcome: str = "merged",
) -> dict[str, Any]:

        steps = [

        ]
    return {
        "task_id": task_id,
        "task_description": task_description,
        "steps": steps,
        "outcome": outcome,
    }

    pairs = normalize_mined_pairs(trajectory)

    assert "+def widget(): pass" in pairs[0]["teacher_text"]
    assert "## Implementation" in pairs[0]["teacher_text"]


def test_normalize_mined_pairs_review_revision_cycle() -> None:
    """A commit-review-commit trajectory produces step_0 + step_1."""

    trajectory = _make_mined_trajectory(steps=[
        {"type": "commit", "description": "V1", "content": "+v1 code"},
        {"type": "review", "description": "Review", "content": "Add error handling"},
        {"type": "commit", "description": "V2", "content": "+v2 with error handling"},
    ])
    pairs = normalize_mined_pairs(trajectory)

    assert len(pairs) == 2

    assert pairs[0]["task_id"] == "pr_owner/repo_42_step_0"
    assert "## Task" in pairs[0]["activation_text"]
    assert "## Implementation" in pairs[0]["teacher_text"]

    assert pairs[1]["task_id"] == "pr_owner/repo_42_step_1"
    assert "## Current Code" in pairs[1]["activation_text"]
    assert "+v1 code" in pairs[1]["activation_text"]
    assert "## Review Feedback" in pairs[1]["activation_text"]
    assert "Add error handling" in pairs[1]["activation_text"]
    assert "## Revision" in pairs[1]["teacher_text"]
    assert "+v2 with error handling" in pairs[1]["teacher_text"]


def test_normalize_mined_pairs_consecutive_commits_uses_last() -> None:
    """Multiple commits before first review: last commit used for step_0."""

    trajectory = _make_mined_trajectory(steps=[
        {"type": "commit", "description": "Draft", "content": "+draft"},
        {"type": "commit", "description": "Polish", "content": "+polished"},
        {"type": "review", "description": "Review", "content": "Fix typo"},
        {"type": "commit", "description": "Fixed", "content": "+fixed"},

    assert len(pairs) == 2

    train, test = split_by_task_id(all_pairs, test_fraction=0.5, seed=42)

    train_ids = {r["task_id"] for r in train}
    test_ids = {r["task_id"] for r in test}
    assert not train_ids & test_ids


def test_normalize_mined_pairs_multiple_rounds() -> None:
    """A complex PR with 3 review rounds produces 4 pairs."""
    from model_training.d2l_data import normalize_mined_pairs

    trajectory = _make_mined_trajectory(steps=[
        {"type": "commit", "description": "V1", "content": "+v1"},

        {"type": "commit", "description": "V3", "content": "+v3"},
        {"type": "review", "description": "R3", "content": "Fix Z"},
        {"type": "commit", "description": "V4", "content": "+v4"},

    pairs = normalize_mined_pairs(trajectory)

    assert len(pairs) == 4  # step_0 + 3 review->revision cycles

    assert pairs[1]["task_id"].endswith("_step_1")
    assert pairs[2]["task_id"].endswith("_step_2")
    assert pairs[3]["task_id"].endswith("_step_3")
    # Each step's "Current Code" is the PREVIOUS step's diff (not cumulative)
    assert "+v2" in pairs[2]["activation_text"]  # step_2 sees v2 as current
    assert "+v1" not in pairs[2]["activation_text"]  # not cumulative

```python
def normalize_mined_pairs(
    trajectory: dict[str, Any],
    compress: bool = True,
    max_diff_lines: int = 500,
    language: str | None = None,
) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    """Convert a mined PR trajectory into per-step training pairs.

    Each review-to-revision cycle becomes one training record with
    activation_text (task + current code + review feedback) and
    teacher_text (activation + revision diff). Compatible with
    ``augment_trajectories``, ``split_by_task_id``, and ``save_jsonl``.

    The algorithm groups contiguous commits and reviews into blocks,
    then pairs each reviews-block with the following commits-block.
    Multiple commits in a block: the last commit is used (the state
    the reviewer actually saw). Multiple reviews: concatenated.

    Args:
        trajectory: Raw mined trajectory from ``mine_pr_diff_chains``.
        compress: Apply diff compression via ``compress_diff``.
        max_diff_lines: Max lines per compressed diff.
        language: Language tag for metadata (from repos config).

    Returns:
        List of training pair records with task_id, activation_text,
        teacher_text, and metadata fields.
    """
    task_id: str = trajectory.get("task_id", "")
    task_desc: str = trajectory.get("task_description", "")
    raw_steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = trajectory.get("steps", [])
    outcome: str = trajectory.get("outcome", "")

    if not raw_steps:
        return []

    # --- Group contiguous runs of commits / reviews ---
    blocks: list[tuple[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]] = []
    cur_type: str | None = None
    cur_block: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    for step in raw_steps:
        stype = step.get("type", "")
        if stype != cur_type:
            if cur_block:
                blocks.append((cur_type or "", cur_block))
            cur_type = stype
            cur_block = [step]
        else:
            cur_block.append(step)
    if cur_block:
        blocks.append((cur_type or "", cur_block))

    def _diff(step: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
        raw = step.get("content", "")
        return compress_diff(raw, max_lines=max_diff_lines) if compress else raw

    def _record(idx: int, activation: str, teacher: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
        return {
            "task_id": f"{task_id}_step_{idx}",
            "activation_text": activation,
            "teacher_text": teacher,
            "metadata": {
                "outcome": outcome,
                "step_index": idx,
                "language": language,
                "source_task_id": task_id,
            },
        }

    records: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
    step_idx = 0
    prev_diff = ""
    bi = 0  # block index

    # --- Step 0: initial commits block ---
    if blocks[0][0] == "commit":
        last_commit = blocks[0][1][-1]
        diff = _diff(last_commit)
        activation = f"## Task\n{task_desc}"
        teacher = f"{activation}\n\n## Implementation\n{diff}"
        records.append(_record(step_idx, activation, teacher))
        prev_diff = diff
        step_idx += 1
        bi = 1

    # --- Subsequent (reviews, commits) pairs ---
    while bi < len(blocks) - 1:
        if blocks[bi][0] == "review" and blocks[bi + 1][0] == "commit":
            review_text = "\n\n".join(
                r.get("content", "") for r in blocks[bi][1]
            )
            last_commit = blocks[bi + 1][1][-1]
            revision = _diff(last_commit)

            activation = f"## Task\n{task_desc}"
            if prev_diff:
                activation += f"\n\n## Current Code\n{prev_diff}"
            activation += f"\n\n## Review Feedback\n{review_text}"
            teacher = f"{activation}\n\n## Revision\n{revision}"

            records.append(_record(step_idx, activation, teacher))
            prev_diff = revision
            step_idx += 1
            bi += 2
        else:
            bi += 1

    return records
```

```python
"""Tests for batch mining mode in mine_github.py."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any
from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch


def _make_config(tmp_path: Path, repos: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None) -> Path:
    """Write a minimal repos config and return its path."""
    config = {
        "defaults": {"max_prs": 5, "quality": False},
        "repos": repos
        or [
            {"repo": "test/alpha", "language": "python"},
            {"repo": "test/beta", "language": "go"},
        ],
    }
    config_path = tmp_path / "repos.json"
    config_path.write_text(json.dumps(config))
    return config_path


def _make_trajectory(repo: str, number: int) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Create a minimal mined trajectory."""
    return {
        "task_id": f"pr_{repo}_{number}",
        "task_description": f"PR #{number}",
        "steps": [
            {"type": "commit", "description": "Init", "content": f"+code_{number}"},
            {"type": "review", "description": "R", "content": "Fix it"},
            {"type": "commit", "description": "Fix", "content": f"+fixed_{number}"},
        ],
        "outcome": "merged",
    }


@patch("model_training.d2l_mining.GitHubClient")
def test_run_batch_produces_per_repo_jsonl(
    mock_client_cls: MagicMock, tmp_path: Path, monkeypatch: Any
) -> None:
    """Batch mode produces one JSONL file per repo."""
    monkeypatch.setenv("GITHUB_TOKEN", "fake-token")
    config_path = _make_config(tmp_path)
    output_dir = tmp_path / "output"

    # Mock mining to return one trajectory per repo
    mock_client = mock_client_cls.return_value
    mock_client.get_paginated.return_value = []

    from model_training.d2l_mining import mine_pr_diff_chains

    with patch(
        "model_training.d2l_mining.mine_pr_diff_chains"
    ) as mock_mine:
        mock_mine.side_effect = lambda repo, **kw: [_make_trajectory(repo, 1)]

        # Import and run batch function
        import importlib
        import sys

        sys.path.insert(0, str(Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "scripts"))
        if "mine_github" in sys.modules:
            importlib.reload(sys.modules["mine_github"])
        import mine_github

        mine_github._run_batch(
            config_path=config_path,
            output_dir=output_dir,
            token="fake-token",
            compress=True,
            max_diff_lines=500,
        )

    # Check output files
    assert (output_dir / "test_alpha.jsonl").exists()
    assert (output_dir / "test_beta.jsonl").exists()

    # Verify JSONL content
    from model_training.d2l_data import load_jsonl

    alpha_records = load_jsonl(output_dir / "test_alpha.jsonl")
    assert len(alpha_records) == 2  # step_0 + step_1 from one trajectory
    assert alpha_records[0]["task_id"] == "pr_test/alpha_1_step_0"
    assert alpha_records[1]["task_id"] == "pr_test/alpha_1_step_1"


def test_mining_repos_config_is_valid_json() -> None:
    """The committed repos config parses without error."""
    config_path = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3] / "instructions" / "mining_repos.json"
    if not config_path.exists():
        return  # skip if not yet created
    config = json.loads(config_path.read_text())
    assert "repos" in config
    assert len(config["repos"]) > 0
    for entry in config["repos"]:
        assert "repo" in entry
        assert "/" in entry["repo"]
```

Replace `scripts/mine_github.py` with the updated version. Changes:
1. `--repo` and `-o` are no longer `required=True`
2. New args: `--batch`, `--output-dir`
3. Existing single-repo logic moved to `_run_single()`
4. New `_run_batch()` function
5. `main()` dispatches based on which args are provided

```python
"""GitHub trajectory mining CLI.

Mines GitHub repositories into trajectory JSON files suitable for
Doc-to-LoRA distillation. Supports single-repo mode (--repo) and
batch mode (--batch) for processing multiple repos from a config.

Usage:
    # Single repo
    uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --repo owner/repo -o out.json
    uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --repo owner/repo --quality -o prs.json

    # Batch mode (produces per-repo JSONL with training pairs)
    uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --batch instructions/mining_repos.json --output-dir data/pairs/
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
import logging
import os
import sys
from pathlib import Path

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s %(levelname)s %(name)s: %(message)s",
)
logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


def parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace:
    """Parse command-line arguments."""
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Mine GitHub repositories into trajectory files.",
        formatter_class=argparse.ArgumentDefaultsHelpFormatter,
    )
    # --- Single-repo mode ---
    parser.add_argument(
        "--repo",
        metavar="OWNER/REPO",
        help='GitHub repository in "owner/repo" format (single-repo mode).',
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "-o",
        "--output",
        type=Path,
        metavar="FILE",
        help="Output JSON file path (single-repo mode).",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--normalize",
        action="store_true",
        help="Normalize trajectories for distillation compatibility (single-repo mode).",
    )
    # --- Batch mode ---
    parser.add_argument(
        "--batch",
        type=Path,
        metavar="CONFIG",
        help="Path to JSON repos config for batch mining.",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--output-dir",
        type=Path,
        metavar="DIR",
        help="Output directory for batch mode (one JSONL per repo).",
    )
    # --- Shared options ---
    parser.add_argument(
        "--mode",
        choices=["prs", "issues", "both"],
        default="both",
        help="Mining mode: pull requests, issues, or both.",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--max",
        type=int,
        default=100,
        dest="max_items",
        metavar="N",
        help="Maximum PRs/issues to process.",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--token",
        default=None,
        metavar="TOKEN",
        help="GitHub personal access token (falls back to GITHUB_TOKEN env var).",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--quality",
        action="store_true",
        help="Pre-filter PRs via GitHub Search API for quality.",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--min-reviews",
        type=int,
        default=1,
        metavar="N",
        help="Minimum review comments for quality filter.",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--min-commits",
        type=int,
        default=2,
        metavar="N",
        help="Minimum commits for quality filter.",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--exclude-labels",
        default=None,
        metavar="L1,L2,...",
        help="Comma-separated labels to exclude.",
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--max-diff-lines",
        type=int,
        default=500,
        metavar="N",
        help="Maximum lines per compressed diff in batch mode.",
    )

    args = parser.parse_args()

    # Validate argument combinations
    if args.batch:
        if not args.output_dir:
            parser.error("--output-dir is required with --batch")
    elif args.repo:
        if not args.output:
            parser.error("-o/--output is required with --repo")
    else:
        parser.error("Either --repo or --batch is required")

    return args


def _run_single(args: argparse.Namespace, token: str) -> None:
    """Run single-repo mining (original behavior)."""
    from model_training.d2l_data import normalize_mined_trajectory
    from model_training.d2l_mining import (
        mine_issue_commit_chains,
        mine_pr_diff_chains,
        search_quality_prs,
    )

    exclude_labels: list[str] | None = None
    if args.exclude_labels:
        exclude_labels = [lbl.strip() for lbl in args.exclude_labels.split(",")]

    trajectories: list[dict] = []

    if args.mode in ("prs", "both"):
        pr_numbers: list[int] | None = None
        if args.quality:
            logger.info(
                "Searching for quality PRs in %s (min_reviews=%d, min_commits=%d)...",
                args.repo,
                args.min_reviews,
                args.min_commits,
            )
            pr_numbers = search_quality_prs(
                args.repo,
                max_results=args.max_items,
                github_token=token,
                min_review_comments=args.min_reviews,
                min_commits=args.min_commits,
                exclude_labels=exclude_labels,
            )
            logger.info("Quality filter selected %d PR(s).", len(pr_numbers))

        logger.info(
            "Mining PR diff chains from %s (max=%d)...", args.repo, args.max_items
        )
        pr_trajectories = mine_pr_diff_chains(
            args.repo,
            max_prs=args.max_items,
            github_token=token,
            pr_numbers=pr_numbers,
        )
        logger.info("Mined %d PR diff chain(s).", len(pr_trajectories))
        trajectories.extend(pr_trajectories)

    if args.mode in ("issues", "both"):
        logger.info(
            "Mining issue-commit chains from %s (max=%d)...",
            args.repo,
            args.max_items,
        )
        issue_trajectories = mine_issue_commit_chains(
            args.repo,
            max_issues=args.max_items,
            github_token=token,
        )
        logger.info("Mined %d issue-commit chain(s).", len(issue_trajectories))
        trajectories.extend(issue_trajectories)

    logger.info("Total trajectories collected: %d.", len(trajectories))

    if args.normalize:
        logger.info("Normalizing %d trajectory/trajectories...", len(trajectories))
        trajectories = [normalize_mined_trajectory(t) for t in trajectories]
        logger.info("Normalization complete.")

    output: Path = args.output
    output.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    with output.open("w", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        json.dump(trajectories, fh, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)
    logger.info("Wrote %d trajectory/trajectories to %s.", len(trajectories), output)


def _run_batch(
    config_path: Path,
    output_dir: Path,
    token: str,
    compress: bool = True,
    max_diff_lines: int = 500,
) -> None:
    """Run batch mining from a repos config file.

    Processes each repo in the config, extracts training pairs via
    normalize_mined_pairs, and saves per-repo JSONL files.
    """
    from model_training.d2l_data import normalize_mined_pairs, save_jsonl
    from model_training.d2l_mining import mine_pr_diff_chains, search_quality_prs

    config = json.loads(config_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    defaults = config.get("defaults", {})
    repos = config.get("repos", [])

    output_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    total_pairs = 0

    for repo_cfg in repos:
        repo = repo_cfg["repo"]
        language = repo_cfg.get("language")
        max_prs = repo_cfg.get("max_prs", defaults.get("max_prs", 50))
        quality = repo_cfg.get("quality", defaults.get("quality", True))
        min_reviews = repo_cfg.get(
            "min_review_comments", defaults.get("min_review_comments", 1)
        )
        min_commits = repo_cfg.get(
            "min_commits", defaults.get("min_commits", 2)
        )

        logger.info("Mining %s (max=%d, quality=%s)...", repo, max_prs, quality)

        pr_numbers: list[int] | None = None
        if quality:
            pr_numbers = search_quality_prs(
                repo,
                max_results=max_prs,
                github_token=token,
                min_review_comments=min_reviews,
                min_commits=min_commits,
            )
            logger.info("Quality filter: %d PRs for %s", len(pr_numbers), repo)

        trajectories = mine_pr_diff_chains(
            repo, max_prs=max_prs, github_token=token, pr_numbers=pr_numbers
        )
        logger.info("Mined %d trajectories from %s", len(trajectories), repo)

        pairs: list[dict] = []
        for traj in trajectories:
            pairs.extend(
                normalize_mined_pairs(
                    traj,
                    compress=compress,
                    max_diff_lines=max_diff_lines,
                    language=language,
                )
            )

        filename = repo.replace("/", "_") + ".jsonl"
        save_jsonl(pairs, output_dir / filename)
        logger.info("Saved %d pairs to %s", len(pairs), filename)
        total_pairs += len(pairs)

    logger.info(
        "Batch complete: %d total pairs across %d repos", total_pairs, len(repos)
    )


def main() -> None:
    """Entry point for the mine_github CLI."""
    args = parse_args()

    token: str | None = args.token or os.environ.get("GITHUB_TOKEN")
    if not token:
        logger.error(
            "No GitHub token. Use --token or set the GITHUB_TOKEN env var."
        )
        sys.exit(1)

    if args.batch:
        _run_batch(
            config_path=args.batch,
            output_dir=args.output_dir,
            token=token,
            max_diff_lines=args.max_diff_lines,
        )
    else:
        _run_single(args, token)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

## Verification Checklist

- [ ] `uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/ -v` -- all tests pass
- [ ] `uv run ruff check libs/model-training/src/ scripts/mine_github.py` -- lint clean
- [ ] `uv run mypy libs/model-training/src/` -- type check clean
- [ ] Single-repo mode still works: `uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --repo fastapi/fastapi --mode prs --max 1 -o /tmp/test.json`
- [ ] Batch mode produces JSONL: `uv run python scripts/mine_github.py --batch instructions/mining_repos.json --output-dir /tmp/pairs/`
- [ ] Output records are compatible with `augment_trajectories()` and `split_by_task_id()`
- [ ] Diff compression removes lockfiles and generated code from training data
- [ ] Metadata includes outcome (merged/closed) and language tags

## Notes

- **DPO negatives:** Currently `search_quality_prs()` only returns merged PRs. For closed-unmerged PRs (DPO negatives), run without `--quality` or add a dedicated search pass later. This is a training-time concern -- the metadata `outcome` field supports it.
- **GitHub rate limits:** Authenticated token gives 5,000 requests/hour. Mining 50 PRs from one repo uses ~200-300 requests (PR list + commits + reviews + commit details). The Phase 1 config (~500 PRs across 10 repos) needs ~2,000-3,000 requests -- fits within one hour.
- **Augmentation:** After mining, run `augment_trajectories()` on the loaded JSONL to generate 3x paraphrased variants before `split_by_task_id()`. This happens on the CPU machine before S3 upload.

72	    or break the task into smaller pieces.
73	
74	    ## Before Reporting Back: Self-Review
75	
76	    Review your work with fresh eyes. Ask yourself:
77	
78	    **Completeness:**
79	    - Did I fully implement everything in the spec?
80	    - Did I miss any requirements?
81	    - Are there edge cases I didn't handle?
82	
83	    **Quality:**
84	    - Is this my best work?
85	    - Are names clear and accurate (match what things do, not how they work)?
86	    - Is the code clean and maintainable?
87	
88	    **Discipline:**
89	    - Did I avoid overbuilding (YAGNI)?
90	    - Did I only build what was requested?
91	    - Did I follow existing patterns in the codebase?
92	
93	    **Testing:**
94	    - Do tests actually verify behavior (not just mock behavior)?
95	    - Did I follow TDD if required?
96	    - Are tests comprehensive?
97	
98	    If you find issues during self-review, fix them now before reporting.
99	
100	    ## Report Format
101	
102	    When done, report:
103	    - **Status:** DONE | DONE_WITH_CONCERNS | BLOCKED | NEEDS_CONTEXT
104	    - What you implemented (or what you attempted, if blocked)
105	    - What you tested and test results
106	    - Files changed
107	    - Self-review findings (if any)
108	    - Any issues or concerns
109	
110	    Use DONE_WITH_CONCERNS if you completed the work but have doubts about correctness.
111	    Use BLOCKED if you cannot complete the task. Use NEEDS_CONTEXT if you need
112	    information that wasn't provided. Never silently produce work you're unsure about.
113	```

\"handler.go\" in result\n    assert \"service_pb2.py\" not in result\n\n\ndef test_compress_diff_filters_binary_extensions() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Binary and media files are removed.\"\"\"\n    from model_training.d2l_diff import compress_diff\n\n    content = (\n        \"--- src/app.ts ---\\n+code\\n\"\n        \"--- assets/logo.png ---\\n+binary\"\n    )\n    result = compress_diff(content)\n    assert \"app.ts\" in result\n    assert \"logo.png\" not in result\n\n\ndef test_compress_diff_filters_build_paths() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Build artifact paths are removed.\"\"\"\n    from model_training.d2l_diff import compress_diff\n\n    content = (\n        \"--- src/lib.rs ---\\n+code\\n\"\n        \"--- dist/bundle.js ---\\n+built\\n\"\n        \"--- node_modules/foo/index.js test_compress_diff_truncates_at_max_lines() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Output respects max_lines limit.\"\"\"\n    from model_training.d2l_diff import compress_diff\n\n    lines = [f\"+line {i}\" for i in # 50 + truncation message\n    assert \"truncated\" in result_lines[-1]\n\n\ndef test_compress_diff_all_filtered_returns_empty() -> None:\n    \"\"\"When all sections are filtered, return empty string.\"\"\"\n    from model_training.d2l_diff import compress_diff\n\n    content = (\n        \"--- package-lock.json ---\\n+lock\\n\"\n        \"--- yarn.lock ---\\n+yarn\\n\"\n        \"--- dist/out.js ---\\n+built\"\n    )\n    result = compress_diff(content)\n    assert result == \"\"\n```\n\n**Step 2: Run tests to verify they fail**\n\nRun: `uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_diff.py -v`\nExpected: FAIL (ModuleNotFoundError)\n\n**Step 3: Implement d2l_diff.py**\n\nCreate `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_diff.py`:\n\n```python\n\"\"\"RTK-style diff compression for training data preparation.\n\nFilters irrelevant files (lockfiles, generated code, binary assets,\nbuild artifacts) and truncates large diffs to minimize token overhead\nin hypernetwork training pairs.\n\nOperates on the concatenated diff format produced by mine_pr_diff_chains:\n    --- src/main.py ---\n    +real code\n    --- package-lock.json ---\n    +lockfile noise\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import PurePosixPath\n\n__all__ = [\"compress_diff\"]\n\n_SKIP_FILENAMES: frozenset[str] = frozenset(\n    {\n        \"package-lock.json\",\n        \"yarn.lock\",\n        \"pnpm-lock.yaml\",\n        \"poetry.lock\",\n        \"uv.lock\",\n        \"Pipfile.lock\",\n        \"Cargo.lock\",\n        \"go.sum\",\n        \"Gemfile.lock\",\n        \"composer.lock\",\n        \"flake.lock\",\n        \".DS_Store\",\n        \".gitattributes\",\n        \".editorconfig\",\n    }\n)\n\n_SKIP_EXTENSIONS: frozenset[str] = frozenset(\n    {\n        \".lock\",\n        \".png\",\n        \".jpg\",\n        \".jpeg\",\n        \".gif\",\n        \".svg\",\n        \".ico\",\n        \".woff\",\n        \".woff2\",\n        \".ttf\",\n        \".eot\",\n        \".mp3\",\n        \".mp4\",\n        \".zip\",\n        \".tar\",\n        \".gz\",\n        \".pdf\",\n        \".pyc\",\n        \".pyo\",\n        \".so\",\n        \".dylib\",\n        \".dll\",\n    }\n)\n\n_SKIP_SUFFIXES: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"_pb2.py\",\n    \"_pb2_grpc.py\",\n    \".pb.go\",\n    \".pb.h\",\n    \".pb.cc\",\n    \".generated.ts\",\n    \".generated.js\",\n    \".g.dart\",\n    \".min.js\",\n    \".min.css\",\n    \".map\",\n)\n\n_SKIP_PATH_SEGMENTS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"node_modules/\",\n    \"__pycache__/\",\n    \".venv/\",\n    \"vendor/\",\n    \"dist/\",\n    \"build/\",\n    \".next/\",\n    \"coverage/\",\n    \".idea/\",\n    \".vscode/\",\n)\n\n_SECTION_RE = re.compile(r\"^(--- .+ ---)$\", re.MULTILINE)\n\n\ndef _should_skip(filename: str) -> bool:\n    \"\"\"Check if a file should be filtered from training data.\"\"\"\n    path = PurePosixPath(filename)\n    if path.name in _SKIP_FILENAMES:\n        return True\n    if path.suffix in _SKIP_EXTENSIONS:\n        return True\n    if any(filename.endswith(s) for s in _SKIP_SUFFIXES):\n        return True\n    return any(seg in filename for seg in _SKIP_PATH_SEGMENTS)\n\n\ndef _truncate(text: str, max_lines: int) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Truncate text to max_lines, appending a marker if truncated.\"\"\"\n    lines = text.split(\"\\n\")\n    if len(lines) <= max_lines:\n        return text\n    kept = lines[:max_lines]\n    kept.append(f\"... ({len(lines) - max_lines} lines truncated)\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(kept)\n\n\ndef compress_diff(content: str, max_lines: int = 500) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Filter irrelevant files and truncate large diffs.\n\n    Parses the ``--- filename ---`` section format produced by\n    ``mine_pr_diff_chains`` and removes lockfiles, generated code,\n    binary files, and build artifacts.\n\n    Args:\n        content: Concatenated diff content with section headers.\n        max_lines: Maximum total output lines.\n\n    Returns:\n        Filtered and truncated diff string.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not content:\n        return content\n\n    parts = _SECTION_RE.split(content)\n\n    # No section headers found -- fall through to truncation only\n    if len(parts) == 1:\n        return _truncate(content, max_lines)\n\n    # parts = [preamble, header1, body1, header2, body2, ...]\n    kept: list[str] = []\n    idx = 1  # skip preamble (parts[0])\n    while idx < len(parts):\n        header = parts[idx]\n        body = parts[idx + 1] if idx + 1 < len(parts) else \"\"\n        filename = header[4:-4]  # strip \"--- \" and \" ---\"\n        if not _should_skip(filename):\n            kept.append(header + body)\n        idx += 2\n\n    result = \"\".join(kept).strip()\n\n    if not result:\n        return \"\"\n\n    return _truncate(result, max_lines)\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run tests to verify pass**\n\nRun: `uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_diff.py -v`\nExpected: 9 passed\n\n**Step 5: Run linting**\n\nRun: `uv run ruff check libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_diff.py libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_diff.py`\nExpected: clean\n\n**Step 6: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_diff.py libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_diff.py\ngit commit -m \"feat: add d2l_diff module for RTK-style diff compression\"\n```\n\n## Context\n\nThis is the first task in a 4-task pipeline for building a GitHub trajectory mining dataset. This module provides diff compression used by Task 2 (normalize_mined_pairs) to filter noise from training data. The module follows the existing `d2l_*` naming convention in the model-training library.\n\nThe project uses `uv run` for ALL Python commands (never bare `python`). Linting uses `ruff`. Tests use `pytest`.\n\n## Before You Begin\n\nIf you have questions about the requirements or approach, ask them now.\n\n## Your Job\n\n1. Write the test file first (TDD)\n2. Run tests to confirm they fail\n3. Write the implementation\n4. Run tests to confirm they pass\n5. Run ruff linting\n6. Commit your work\n7. Report back\n\nWork from: /Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune\n\n## Self-Review\n\nBefore reporting, check:\n- Did I implement exactly what the spec says?\n- Do all 9 tests pass?\n- Is ruff clean?\n- Did I commit?\n\n## Report Format\n\nWhen done, report:\n- **Status:** DONE | DONE_WITH_CONCERNS | BLOCKED | NEEDS_CONTEXT\n- What you implemented\n- Test results\n- Files changed\n- Any concerns"
}

Counting: _pb2.py, _pb2_grpc.py, .pb.go, .pb.h, .pb.cc, .generated.ts, .generated.js, .g.dart, .min.js, .min.css, .map = **11** ✅

Note: `_SKIP_SUFFIXES` is declared as `tuple[str, ...]` rather than `frozenset[str]`. The spec says "Filter frozensets" for all four. This is a minor deviation — it's a tuple, not a frozenset. Same for `_SKIP_PATH_SEGMENTS`.

**`_SKIP_PATH_SEGMENTS` (spec: 10 items)**
Counting: node_modules/, __pycache__/, .venv/, vendor/, dist/, build/, .next/, coverage/, .idea/, .vscode/ = **10** ✅ (also tuple, not frozenset)

**`_SECTION_RE`**
`re.compile(r"^(--- .+ ---)$", re.MULTILINE)` ✅

**`_should_skip(filename)`** — uses `PurePosixPath`, checks name, suffix, suffixes via `endswith`, path segments via `in` ✅

**`_truncate(text, max_lines)`** — splits on `\n`, truncates, appends marker ✅

**`compress_diff` algorithm:**
1. Returns empty content unchanged (`if not content: return content`) ✅
2. Falls through to truncation if no sections (`len(parts) == 1`) ✅
3. Parses via `_SECTION_RE.split(content)` ✅
4. Filters sections by filename ✅
5. Reassembles kept sections ✅
6. Truncates to max_lines ✅

**Test functions (spec: 9 named tests)**
1. `test_compress_diff_empty_input` ✅
2. `test_compress_diff_no_sections_truncates` ✅
3. `test_compress_diff_keeps_source_files` ✅
4. `test_compress_diff_filters_lockfiles` ✅
5. `test_compress_diff_filters_generated_code` ✅
6. `test_compress_diff_filters_binary_extensions` ✅
7. `test_compress_diff_filters_build_paths` ✅
8. `test_compress_diff_truncates_at_max_lines` ✅
9. `test_compress_diff_all_filtered_returns_empty` ✅

All 9 test names match exactly, and each test exercises its stated behavior with appropriate assertions.

**Verdict: ✅ Spec compliant**

One minor note: `_SKIP_SUFFIXES` and `_SKIP_PATH_SEGMENTS` are declared as `tuple[str, ...]` rather than `frozenset[str]`. The spec groups all four under "Filter frozensets," but this is functionally inconsequential — the lookup behavior is correct (`endswith` for suffixes, `in` string membership for path segments). The counts (11 and 10), item contents, and all algorithm logic are exactly as specified.

---

## Code Review: d2l_diff.py -- RTK-style Diff Compression Module

### Summary

This is a clean, well-structured, self-contained module that does one thing well. 9 tests pass, mypy is clean, ruff lint is clean, and 100% branch/statement coverage on the module. The implementation follows existing project conventions closely. Good work.

### Plan Alignment

The original plan at `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-03-31-data-mining-implementation.md` does not contain a d2l_diff task -- the plan covers httpx dependency, GitHubClient, mine_pr_diff_chains, mine_issue_commit_chains, outcome normalization, and a CLI script. This module is an addition to the plan scope, likely identified as needed once mined diff data was being produced. This is a **justified deviation** -- the module sits naturally between the mining output (which produces `--- filename ---` section-delimited diffs) and the training data normalization step. It fills a real gap: raw mined diffs contain lockfiles, binary blobs, and build artifacts that would waste tokens during hypernetwork training.

### What Was Done Well

- **Single responsibility.** One module, one public function (`compress_diff`), one clear job. The `__all__` export is correct and minimal.
- **Follows d2l_* naming convention.** Consistent with d2l_config, d2l_data, d2l_mining, d2l_prep, d2l_probe, d2l_lora, d2l_train.
- **Correct format coupling.** The `--- filename ---` regex matches the exact format produced by `d2l_mining.py` lines 194 and 309 (`f"--- {f['filename']} ---\n{patch}"`).
- **Deferred-import-friendly.** The module has no GPU or heavy imports (only `re` and `pathlib.PurePosixPath`), which is ideal since it needs to run in CPU-only CI. This follows the INFRA-05 pattern correctly.
- **Defensive coding.** Handles empty input, no-section-headers fallback, all-sections-filtered, and bounds the output with `max_lines`.
- **Good test coverage.** 100% statement and branch coverage per the pytest-cov output. Each test targets a specific filter category (lockfiles, generated code, binary extensions, build paths, truncation, empty, all-filtered).
- **Type annotations are complete.** Mypy passes with no issues.

### Issues

**Important (should fix):**

1. **`ruff format` failure on test file.** Running `uv run ruff format --check` shows the test file would be reformatted. Several multi-line string concatenations in tests (`content = ("...\n" "...")`) do not match ruff's preferred formatting. This will fail CI's ruff format check.

   File: `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_diff.py`

   Fix: Run `uv run ruff format libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_diff.py`.

2. **Missing `__init__.py` deferred-import comment.** Every other d2l_* module is documented in the deferred-import comment block in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/__init__.py` (lines 13-37). `d2l_diff` is not mentioned there. While it does not need a GPU-deferred import (it has no heavy deps), the comment block serves as a discoverable index of all d2l_* modules. Adding a line like `#   from model_training.d2l_diff import compress_diff` keeps the convention consistent and helps future developers find the module.

**Suggestions (nice to have):**

3. **Redundant entries in `_SKIP_FILENAMES`.** Six of the entries in `_SKIP_FILENAMES` (yarn.lock, poetry.lock, uv.lock, Cargo.lock, Gemfile.lock, flake.lock) have the `.lock` suffix, which is already caught by `_SKIP_EXTENSIONS`. The explicit filenames in `_SKIP_FILENAMES` are therefore redundant for those six. Only `package-lock.json` (.json suffix), `pnpm-lock.yaml` (.yaml suffix), `go.sum` (.sum suffix), `.DS_Store`, `.gitattributes`, and `.editorconfig` are uniquely needed in `_SKIP_FILENAMES`. This is not a bug -- the redundancy is harmless and arguably makes the intent clearer -- but worth noting if the lists are ever refactored.

4. **No test for the `_should_skip` function directly.** All tests go through `compress_diff`, which means the filter logic is tested indirectly. A few direct unit tests for `_should_skip` with edge-case filenames (e.g., `"src/foo.min.js"`, `"vendor/lib/code.py"`, `"src/real.lock"`) would make it easier to verify and extend the filter rules in isolation. This is optional given the current 100% coverage.

5. **Preamble content is silently discarded.** In `compress_diff`, `parts[0]` (any text before the first `--- filename ---` header) is skipped without any logging or indication. This is the correct behavior for the known input format (d2l_mining does not produce preambles), but if this module is ever used with raw git diff output or other formats that have leading metadata, the silent discard could be surprising. A brief inline comment at line 144 explaining why the preamble is skipped would be sufficient.

### Verdict

This is solid, well-scoped work. The two important items to address before merging are: (1) run `ruff format` on the test file, and (2) add the module to the `__init__.py` deferred-import comment block. Both are trivial fixes.

Multiple reviews: concatenated.\n\n    Args:\n        trajectory: Raw mined trajectory from ``mine_pr_diff_chains``.\n        compress: Apply diff compression via ``compress_diff``.\n        max_diff_lines: Max lines per compressed diff.\n        language: Language tag for metadata (from repos config).\n\n    Returns:\n        List of training pair records with task_id, activation_text,\n        teacher_text, and metadata fields.\n    \"\"\"\n    task_id: str = trajectory.get(\"task_id\", \"\")\n    task_desc: str = trajectory.get(\"task_description\", \"\")\n    raw_steps: list[dict[str, Any]] = trajectory.get(\"steps\", [])\n    outcome: str = trajectory.get(\"outcome\", \"\")\n\n    if not raw_steps:\n        return []\n\n    # --- Group contiguous runs of commits / reviews ---\n    blocks: list[tuple[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]] = []\n    cur_type: str | None = None\n    cur_block: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    for step in raw_steps:\n        stype = step.get(\"type\", \"\")\n        if stype != cur_type:\n            if cur_block:\n                blocks.append((cur_type or \"\", cur_block))\n            cur_type = stype\n            cur_block = [step]\n        else:\n            cur_block.append(step)\n    if cur_block:\n        blocks.append((cur_type or \"\", cur_block))\n\n    def _diff(step: dict[str, Any]) -> str:\n        raw = step.get(\"content\", \"\")\n        return compress_diff(raw, max_lines=max_diff_lines) if compress else raw\n\n    def _record(idx: int, activation: str, teacher: str) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        return {\n            \"task_id\": f\"{task_id}_step_{idx}\",\n            \"activation_text\": activation,\n            \"teacher_text\": teacher,\n            \"metadata\": {\n                \"outcome\": outcome,\n                \"step_index\": idx,\n                \"language\": language,\n                \"source_task_id\": task_id,\n            },\n        }\n\n    records: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n    step_idx = 0\n    prev_diff = \"\"\n    bi = 0  # block index\n\n    # --- Step 0: initial commits block ---\n    if blocks[0][0] == \"commit\":\n        last_commit = blocks[0][1][-1]\n        diff = _diff(last_commit)\n        activation = f\"## Task\\n{task_desc}\"\n        teacher = f\"{activation}\\n\\n## Implementation\\n{diff}\"\n        records.append(_record(step_idx, activation, teacher))\n        prev_diff = diff\n        step_idx += 1\n        bi = 1\n\n    # --- Subsequent (reviews, commits) pairs ---\n    while bi < len(blocks) - 1:\n        if blocks[bi][0] == \"review\" and blocks[bi + 1][0] == \"commit\":\n            review_text = \"\\n\\n\".join(\n                r.get(\"content\", \"\") for r in blocks[bi][1]\n            )\n            last_commit = blocks[bi + 1][1][-1]\n            revision = _diff(last_commit)\n\n            activation = f\"## Task\\n{task_desc}\"\n            if prev_diff:\n                activation += f\"\\n\\n## Current Code\\n{prev_diff}\"\n            activation += f\"\\n\\n## Review Feedback\\n{review_text}\"\n            teacher = f\"{activation}\\n\\n## Revision\\n{revision}\"\n\n            records.append(_record(step_idx, activation, teacher))\n            prev_diff = revision\n            step_idx += 1\n            bi += 2\n        else:\n            bi += 1\n\n    return records\n```\n\n**Step 4: Run tests to verify pass**\n\nRun: `uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py -k \"normalize_mined_pairs\" -v`\nExpected: 9 passed\n\n**Step 5: Run full test suite + linting**\n\nRun: `uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/ -v && uv run ruff check libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py && uv run ruff format --check libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py`\nExpected: All pass + lint clean + format clean\n\n**Step 6: Commit**\n\n```bash\ngit add libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py\ngit commit -m \"feat: add normalize_mined_pairs for per-step training pair extraction\"\n```\n\n## Context\n\nThis is Task 2 of 4. It depends on Task 1 (d2l_diff.py) which is already committed. ```
Now I have a complete picture. Here is my review.

---

## Code Review: normalize_mined_pairs (Task 2)

### What Was Done Well

- The core contiguous-block grouping algorithm is correct and elegant. The `_group_steps_into_blocks` function correctly handles all combinations of interleaved commit/review sequences, and the `prev_diff` chain accurately propagates the most recent revision through subsequent steps.
- Extracting `_group_steps_into_blocks` and `_make_pair_record` as separate functions is a good call -- it keeps `normalize_mined_pairs` itself under the ruff C901 complexity threshold and improves testability.
- The structured section headers (`## Task`, `## Current Code`, `## Review Feedback`, `## Revision`, `## Implementation`) create a clean, parseable format that will work well as activation/teacher content for hypernetwork training.
- The `compress` and `max_diff_lines` parameters provide appropriate control over diff filtering, and the delegation to the existing `compress_diff` function is clean.
- All 23 tests pass, ruff is clean, and test coverage for the new code is solid at 92%.
- The output records include the three keys (`task_id`, `activation_text`, `teacher_text`) needed by `augment_trajectories` and `save_jsonl`, plus a `metadata` dict that downstream consumers can safely ignore.

### Issues

#### Important: `split_by_task_id` task-family leakage with `_step_N` suffix

This is the most significant issue in the implementation. The `_make_pair_record` function generates task_ids like `pr_a/b_1_step_0` and `pr_a/b_1_step_1`. When these are passed to `split_by_task_id`, each step is treated as an independent task_id. This means steps from the same PR can end up in different splits -- `_step_0` in train and `_step_1` in test.

I verified this empirically:

```
task_ids: ['pr_a/b_1_step_0', 'pr_a/b_1_step_1', 'pr_c/d_2_step_0']
train_ids: {'pr_a/b_1_step_0', 'pr_c/d_2_step_0'}
test_ids: {'pr_a/b_1_step_1'}
All of pr_a/b_1 in same split? False
```

This defeats the purpose of `split_by_task_id`, which exists specifically to prevent task-family leakage. Step 1's activation contains the code from step 0, so if step 0 is in train and step 1 is in test, the model could memorize the progression pattern and inflate test metrics.

The existing test at line 708 (`test_normalize_mined_pairs_compatible_with_split_by_task_id`) only asserts that there is no literal overlap between train_ids and test_ids, which is trivially true since every step has a unique suffix. It does not test the actual semantic requirement -- that all steps from the same source PR land in the same split.

There are two reasonable fixes:
1. Use the source `task_id` (without `_step_N`) as the `task_id` field, and put the step index only in metadata. This makes `split_by_task_id` group all steps from one PR together automatically. Downstream code that needs unique record identifiers can concatenate `task_id + metadata["step_index"]`.
2. Keep the current `_step_N` task_ids, but document that callers must split on `metadata["source_task_id"]` rather than `task_id`. This requires a custom split function or pre-grouping step.

Option 1 is strongly preferred because it works with the existing `split_by_task_id` without any special handling.

#### Important: Test numbering comment is incorrect

At line 577 in `test_d2l_data.py`:
```python
# Test 11-19: normalize_mined_pairs
```

The existing file already has tests numbered 11 through 14 for `normalize_mined_trajectory` (lines 377-545). The new tests would logically be 15-23 (there are 9 new tests). The "11-19" label conflicts with the existing test numbering scheme. This is a minor documentation issue but will cause confusion as the test file grows.

#### Suggestion: Add a test for `compress=False`

All tests use the default `compress=True`. Since the compress parameter is part of the public API and changes behavior (I verified: with compression, lockfile content gets stripped; without, it passes through raw), there should be at least one test exercising `compress=False` to lock in that behavior.

#### Suggestion: Add a test for review-first trajectories

The implementation handles trajectories that start with a review block (no initial commit) -- it correctly pairs the leading review with the first commit block. But there is no test covering this path. Since real PR data could have this shape (e.g., a reviewer comments before any code is pushed, or the initial commit was squashed away), it would be worth adding a test.

#### Suggestion: The `_make_mined_trajectory` helper steps type annotation is slightly imprecise

The helper declares `steps: list[dict[str, str]] | None = None`, but the actual mined trajectory steps can have `dict[str, Any]` values (e.g., if a future field is numeric). This is minor and does not affect correctness today, but using `list[dict[str, Any]]` would be more future-proof and consistent with the type used elsewhere in the file.

### The mypy finding

The mypy error (`Cannot find implementation or library stub for module named "inference.ollama_provider"`) is a pre-existing issue in the `augment_trajectories` function, not introduced by this change. The new code introduces no new mypy errors.

### Summary

The implementation is solid and the algorithm is correct. The one important issue is the `_step_N` task_id suffix creating task-family leakage through `split_by_task_id`. This needs to be addressed before the pipeline is used for actual training, as it would compromise the integrity of train/test evaluation. The fix is straightforward -- use the base task_id as the record's `task_id` and relegate the step index to metadata only.

**Files reviewed:**
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` (lines 658-779: new code)
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py` (lines 548-759: new code)
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_diff.py` (supporting module)

Done. Here's a summary of what was changed:

**Source fix** (`libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py`):
- `_make_pair_record`: Changed `"task_id": f"{task_id}_step_{idx}"` to `"task_id": task_id`. The step index stays in `metadata["step_index"]` only. This ensures `split_by_task_id` sees one task_id per PR, not one per step, eliminating cross-split leakage.

**Test fixes** (`libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py`):
- `test_normalize_mined_pairs_single_commit_produces_step0`: task_id assertion → `"pr_owner/repo_42"`, added `metadata["step_index"] == 0` check.
- `test_normalize_mined_pairs_review_revision_cycle`: Both step task_ids assert base id, plus step_index 0/1 in metadata.
- `test_normalize_mined_pairs_trailing_reviews_skipped`: task_id assertion updated + step_index check added.
- `test_normalize_mined_pairs_multiple_rounds`: Replaced `endswith("_step_N")` with `metadata["step_index"] == N`.
- `test_normalize_mined_pairs_compatible_with_split_by_task_id`: Semantic rewrite — now verifies no `source_task_id` overlap between train/test splits (the actual no-leakage guarantee), not just task_id set disjointness.
- Fixed stale comment "Test 11-19" → "Tests 15-22".

All 23 tests pass, ruff clean.

- `--repo` and `-o` are no longer required (no `required=True`). Correct.
- New args added: `--batch`, `--output-dir`, `--max-diff-lines`. All present with correct types/defaults.
- `_run_single(args, token)` is intact with original behavior (quality filtering, mode, normalize, output).
- `_run_batch(config_path, output_dir, token, compress=True, max_diff_lines=500)` — correct signature.
- `_run_batch` reads config/defaults correctly, calls `search_quality_prs` conditionally, calls `mine_pr_diff_chains`, calls `normalize_mined_pairs` and `save_jsonl` correctly.
- Validation logic: `--batch` requires `--output-dir`; `--repo` requires `-o`; one of the two modes is required. Correct.
- `main()` dispatches on `args.batch`. Correct.

**`libs/model-training/tests/test_mine_batch.py`**
- Both helpers `_make_config()` and `_make_trajectory()` present.
- `test_run_batch_produces_per_repo_jsonl`: loads the script via `importlib`, patches `model_training.d2l_mining.mine_pr_diff_chains` at the right level (deferred import pattern), verifies both `test_alpha.jsonl` and `test_beta.jsonl` are created, asserts `len == 2` (correct: 3-step trajectory [commit, review, commit] → 2 training records), checks `task_id` and `metadata` fields.
- `test_mining_repos_config_is_valid_json`: validates the real `mining_repos.json` exists, parses as JSON, has `repos` with at least 1 entry, and every entry has `repo` with `/`. Correct.

No extra features beyond spec, no regressions identified.

I have a thorough picture now. Let me compile the full review.

---

## Code Review: Task 3 -- Batch Mining CLI + Repos Config

### What Was Done Well

- The refactoring from a monolithic `main()` into `_run_single()` and `_run_batch()` is clean and well-organized. The separation of concerns is clear.
- Argument validation in `parse_args()` uses `parser.error()` properly, giving helpful error messages for missing companion flags.
- The JSON config structure (`mining_repos.json`) is simple and effective -- defaults with per-repo overrides.
- Deferred imports (inside function bodies) follow the project's INFRA-05 pattern correctly.
- Tests pass. Lint and type-checking pass on the Python files.
- Logging throughout the batch loop is thorough and actionable.

### Plan Alignment

The formal plan document (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-03-31-data-mining-implementation.md`) does not include a "Task 3: Repos Config + Batch Mining CLI" -- that plan's task numbering covers the original mining implementation (GitHubClient, mining functions, normalization, CLI script). The batch mode appears to have been specified in the task system outside the formal plan. This is a reasonable addition that builds on the existing CLI, so the deviation from the plan document is a benign extension, not a problematic departure.

### Issues

#### Important (should fix)

**1. `_run_batch` silently ignores `--mode` and `--exclude-labels` CLI flags.**

In `_run_single()`, both `args.mode` and `args.exclude_labels` are honored. In `_run_batch()`, they are not read from `args` at all -- the function only does PR mining and never issues mining, and `exclude_labels` is never parsed or passed to `search_quality_prs`. The config JSON also has a `"mode": "prs"` default that is never read by the code.

This means a user who passes `--mode both --exclude-labels bot,ci --batch config.json --output-dir out/` would silently get PRs-only with no label exclusion.

There are two reasonable fixes: (a) read `mode` and `exclude_labels` from the per-repo config / defaults and honor them in the batch loop, or (b) document clearly that batch mode is PR-only and remove the dead `"mode"` field from the config. Given the config already has `"mode": "prs"` in defaults, option (a) is more consistent.

**2. `_run_batch` does not handle per-repo errors gracefully.**

If `mine_pr_diff_chains` or `search_quality_prs` raises an exception for one repo (network error, 404, unexpected API response), the entire batch aborts. For a batch of 10 repos (~500 PRs), this is a significant resilience gap. A try/except around the per-repo body that logs the error and continues to the next repo would be appropriate.

**3. The test's `importlib.util` approach for importing the script is fragile.**

`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/tests/test_mine_batch.py` lines 51-59 use `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` with a relative path traversal (`Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]`) to import the script. This works but has two problems:
- It inserts the module into `sys.modules["mine_github"]` as a side effect, potentially contaminating other tests if run in the same process.
- The path traversal depth (`.parents[3]`) is brittle if the test file ever moves.

A cleaner approach would be to add `scripts/` to `sys.path` temporarily and import, or better yet, extract `_run_batch` into the `model_training` package (it already depends entirely on `model_training` functions) so it can be imported normally. The script would become a thin CLI wrapper.

**4. The mock in `test_run_batch_produces_per_repo_jsonl` patches the wrong target.**

Line 40 patches `model_training.d2l_mining.mine_pr_diff_chains`, but `_run_batch()` imports it as `from model_training.d2l_mining import mine_pr_diff_chains` at call time inside the function body. Because the import happens each time `_run_batch` is called, and the import uses `from ... import`, the name is re-bound from the module attribute. The patch works here only because `@patch` modifies the module-level attribute before `_run_batch` does its deferred import, and `from X import Y` reads the already-patched attribute. This is correct behavior for `unittest.mock.patch`, but the test does not patch `search_quality_prs`, meaning the quality filter code path is untested. The config has `"quality": False` which sidesteps it, but if the default ever changes, the test will make real API calls.

#### Suggestions (nice to have)

**5. The `--batch` and `--repo` flags can technically both be provided.**

The validation logic in `parse_args()` checks `if args.batch:` first, then `elif args.repo:`. If both are provided, `--batch` wins silently. A mutual exclusion check (or `argparse` mutually exclusive group) would be more explicit:

```python
group = parser.add_mutually_exclusive_group(required=True)
group.add_argument("--repo", ...)
group.add_argument("--batch", ...)
```

**6. The config file `mining_repos.json` lives in `instructions/` which is an unusual location.**

The project's `instructions/` directory appears to be for human documentation. A `data/` or `config/` directory might be more conventional for machine-read configuration. This is minor.

**7. The `compress` parameter in `_run_batch` is not exposed as a CLI flag.**

`_run_batch` accepts `compress: bool = True` but the CLI always passes the default. If a user wants uncompressed output, there is no way to request it from the command line.

**8. No test for the `_run_single` path in isolation.**

The review criteria asked whether `_run_single` exactly preserves the original behavior. Comparing the diff, the logic is preserved line-for-line with two cosmetic changes: some comments were removed, and a line-wrapping change in the `mine_issue_commit_chains` logger call. Functionally identical. However, there is no test that exercises `_run_single` -- only `_run_batch` is tested. Adding a corresponding test for single-repo mode would give confidence that the refactoring did not break the original path.

### Summary

The implementation is solid in its core structure. The two Important items that deserve attention before merging are: (1) the silent discard of `--mode` and `--exclude-labels` in batch mode, and (2) the lack of per-repo error handling in the batch loop. Items 3 and 4 about test structure are worth addressing but not blocking.

### Relevant Files

- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/scripts/mine_github.py` -- main implementation
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/instructions/mining_repos.json` -- repos config
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/tests/test_mine_batch.py` -- tests
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` -- normalize_mined_pairs and save_jsonl used by batch mode
- `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/rune/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-03-31-data-mining-implementation.md` -- original plan (does not include batch mode as a formal task)

- **Bucket Name Format:** `{project-name}-{aws-account-id}-{aws-region}-artifacts`

### Usage in GPU Development Environment

The artifacts bucket is used extensively in GPU devbox deployment (`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/infra/providers/aws/files/gpu-userdata.sh`):

- **Workspace Snapshots:** S3 path format: `s3://{artifact-bucket}/workspace-snapshots/{environment}/latest/`

- **Snapshot Metadata:** Stores `.sync-metadata` file with sync timestamps
- **Exclusions:** Git metadata, Python caches, node_modules, and other build artifacts are excluded from snapshots
- **Shutdown Hook:** Workspace is automatically synced to S3 before instance termination (protects against spot interruptions)

2. Or tell me where to find it

On your database/streaming question — good instinct, but I'd say **not yet**. Here's why:

**Phase 1 scale (~1500 pairs, ~50MB):** JSONL + S3 is the right call. The entire dataset loads into memory trivially. The existing `load_jsonl` → `augment_trajectories` → `split_by_task_id` pipeline handles it cleanly.

**When to reconsider (Phase 2+):**
- If you need **curriculum learning** (feed easier pairs first, harder later) — then you need query-by-difficulty
- If you need **incremental mining** (track which PRs you already mined, avoid re-work) — a `mined_prs` table helps
- If dataset grows to **10K+ pairs** — switch JSONL → Parquet on S3. HuggingFace `datasets` can stream Parquet lazily without loading all into memory
- If you need **lineage tracking** (which pairs contributed to which adapters) — DuckDB is perfect for this

**The natural upgrade path would be:**
1. JSONL on S3 (now) → works for Phase 1
2. Parquet on S3 + HuggingFace `datasets` streaming (Phase 2) → handles 10K+ pairs, columnar queries, lazy loading
3. DuckDB catalog (Phase 3, if needed) → full queryability, lineage, deduplication

The adapter registry already uses SQLite for tracking. A similar pattern for dataset provenance would be clean but premature right now. KISS — start with JSONL, upgrade when you feel the pain.

> AGENT

E2E pipeline works end-to-end:

- **Step 0:** Initial commit → `## Implementation` (515 chars of diff)
- **Steps 1-4:** Four review→revision cycles, each with `## Current Code` + `## Review Feedback` + `## Revision` — exactly the structure the hypernetwork needs
- **Real review content:** Dutch translation PR with substantive inline code suggestions
- **No leakage:** All 5 steps from same PR stay together in split
- **Diff compression working:** No lockfiles or generated code in output

The pipeline is fully functional. For S3 upload, based on the infra repo, your artifacts bucket follows the pattern `{project}-{account-id}-{region}-artifacts`. The training data would go to:

```bash
aws s3 sync /path/to/pairs/ s3://{your-artifacts-bucket}/training-data/github-pairs/
```

The full Phase 1 mining (~500 PRs, 10 repos) should take about 15-20 minutes given the ~10 API requests per PR and the 5,000/hr rate limit.

## AWS Configuration & DevPod Setup Report

Based on my comprehensive search of the infrastructure code, here's the complete configuration for creating an S3 bucket accessible from the GPU devpod:

---

### **AWS Account & Region Information**

| Parameter | Value |
|-----------|-------|
| **AWS Region** | `eu-west-2` (London) |
| **AWS Account ID** | `949678234935` |
| **Project Name** | `elixirtrials` |
| **Environment** | `dev` |
| **Terraform State Bucket** | `elixirtrials-infra-state-eu-west-2` |

**Local AWS Configuration:**
- Profile: `AdministratorAccess-949678234935`
- SSO Session: `Infra`
- SSO Start URL: `https://elixirtrials.awsapps.com/start`

---

### **DevPod IAM Configuration**

**GPU Instance IAM Role:**
- **Role Name**: `elixirtrials-dev-gpu-instance`
- **Instance Profile**: `elixirtrials-dev-gpu-instance`
- **Role ARN**: Referenced in IAM stack outputs as `gpu_instance_role_arn`
- **Instance Profile ARN**: Referenced in IAM stack outputs as `gpu_instance_profile_arn`

**IAM Policy: S3 Data Access**
- **Policy Name**: `elixirtrials-dev-s3-data-access`
- **Description**: "Access to S3 artifact bucket for ML data, adapters, and checkpoints"
- **Permissions**:
  - `s3:GetObject`
  - `s3:PutObject`
  - `s3:ListBucket`
  - `s3:DeleteObject`
- **Resource**: Applies to the artifact bucket and all objects within it

**Additional IAM Policies Attached to GPU Role:**
1. `AmazonSSMManagedInstanceCore` (AWS managed) - for SSM Session Manager access
2. `CloudWatchAgentServerPolicy` (AWS managed) - for CloudWatch monitoring
3. `elixirtrials-dev-ecr-access` - ECR pull/push permissions
4. `elixirtrials-dev-secrets-read` - Secrets Manager read access

---

### **Existing S3 Bucket**

**Artifacts Bucket:**
- **Name Pattern**: `{project_name}-{account_id}-{region}-artifacts`
- **Actual Name**: `elixirtrials-949678234935-eu-west-2-artifacts`
- **ARN**: `arn:aws:s3:::elixirtrials-949678234935-eu-west-2-artifacts`
- **Features**:
  - Server-side encryption (AES256)
  - Versioning enabled
  - Lifecycle policies:
    - Temporary files in `tmp/` expire after 7 days
    - Checkpoints in `checkpoints/` transition to STANDARD_IA after 30 days
  - All public access blocked

**S3 Usage in GPU Devpod:**
The devpod uses S3 for workspace snapshots via the `workspace-sync` script:
- **Path**: `s3://{artifact_bucket}/workspace-snapshots/{environment}/`
- **Region**: Passed to `aws s3` commands via the `AWS_REGION` variable
- **Auto-Sync**: Every 30 minutes via cron and at shutdown via systemd

---

### **How GPU Devpod Gets AWS Access**

**Credentials Method:**
- **IAM Instance Profile**: Attached directly to the EC2 instance via the `iam_instance_profile` parameter in compute stack
- **No explicit credentials needed**: Instance assumes the role automatically via EC2 metadata service

**In the GPU Userdata Script** (`gpu-userdata.sh`):
- Variables injected: `artifact_bucket`, `aws_region`, `environment`, `project_name`
- The `workspace-sync` script uses the instance's IAM role (not explicit credentials)
- Commands: `aws s3 sync` and `aws s3 cp` work directly without credential configuration

---

### **S3 Bucket Naming Convention**

**Format:**
```
{project_name}-{account_id}-{region}-{purpose}
```

**Examples:**
- Artifacts: `elixirtrials-949678234935-eu-west-2-artifacts`
- Terraform State: `elixirtrials-infra-state-eu-west-2`

**Components:**
- `project_name`: "elixirtrials" (from variables)
- `account_id`: "949678234935" (retrieved via `data.aws_caller_identity.current.account_id`)
- `region`: "eu-west-2" (from `var.aws_region`)
- `purpose`: Suffix indicating bucket role (artifacts, state, etc.)

---

### **Key Terraform Files & Outputs**

| File Path | Purpose |
|-----------|---------|
| `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/infra/providers/aws/stacks/storage/main.tf` | S3 artifact bucket definition |
| `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/infra/providers/aws/stacks/iam/main.tf` | GPU instance role & S3 policy |
| `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/infra/providers/aws/stacks/compute/main.tf` | GPU EC2 instance with IAM profile |
| `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/infra/providers/aws/files/gpu-userdata.sh` | Bootstrap script with workspace-sync |
| `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/infra/providers/aws/environments/dev.tfvars` | Environment variables (region, account, project) |

**Outputs to Reference:**
- `artifact_bucket_name` - the S3 bucket name
- `artifact_bucket_arn` - the bucket ARN
- `gpu_instance_profile_name` - the instance profile name
- `gpu_instance_role_arn` - the IAM role ARN

---

### **To Create an S3 Bucket Accessible from the GPU DevPod**

1. **Follow the naming convention**: `elixirtrials-949678234935-eu-west-2-{purpose}`
2. **Add to the storage stack** (`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/infra/providers/aws/stacks/storage/main.tf`):
   - Create a new `aws_s3_bucket` resource
   - Attach the same security policies (versioning, encryption, public access block)
3. **Grant access via IAM policy** in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/infra/providers/aws/stacks/iam/main.tf`:
   - Add the bucket ARN to an existing or new S3 policy
   - Ensure the GPU instance role has the policy attached
4. **The GPU instance will automatically have access** through its IAM role

---

2	# GPU instance user-data bootstrap script
3	# Installs: Docker Engine, NVIDIA Container Toolkit, CloudWatch Agent, uv, rune
4	# Runs on first boot via cloud-init on Deep Learning Base GPU AMI (Ubuntu 22.04)
5	#
6	# NOTE: This file is processed by OpenTofu templatefile(). All literal dollar-brace
7	# in bash must be doubled ($$) so templatefile passes them through.
8	#
9	# shellcheck disable=SC2154  # Variables are injected by OpenTofu templatefile()
10	set -euxo pipefail
11	export DEBIAN_FRONTEND=noninteractive
12	
13	# Variables injected by OpenTofu templatefile()
14	ENVIRONMENT="${environment}"
15	PROJECT_NAME="${project_name}"
16	ARTIFACT_BUCKET="${artifact_bucket}"
17	AWS_REGION="${aws_region}"
18	WORKSPACE_S3_PREFIX="s3://$ARTIFACT_BUCKET/workspace-snapshots/$ENVIRONMENT"
19	
20	echo "=== Bootstrapping GPU devbox for $PROJECT_NAME ($ENVIRONMENT) ==="
21	
22	# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
23	# Verify NVIDIA drivers (pre-installed in Deep Learning Base GPU AMI)
24	# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
25	if command -v nvidia-smi &> /dev/null; then
26	    echo "NVIDIA driver detected:"
27	    nvidia-smi --query-gpu=name,driver_version,memory.total --format=csv,noheader
28	else
29	    echo "WARNING: nvidia-smi not found. GPU drivers may not be installed."
30	fi
31	
32	# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
33	# Docker Engine
34	# -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
35	echo "=== Installing Docker Engine ==="
36	apt-get update
37	apt-get install -y jq curl ca-certificates gnupg lsb-release git make
38	
39	# Install Docker CE if not already present (DLAMI may have containerd pre-installed)
40	if ! command -v docker &> /dev/null; then
```

> AGENT

Here's what's set up:

**Two complementary mechanisms:**

1. **SSM Parameter** (already created): `/elixirtrials/dev/training-data-path`
   - Readable from anywhere (local dev, CI, other services)
   - `aws ssm get-parameter --name /elixirtrials/dev/training-data-path --query Parameter.Value --output text --region eu-west-2`

2. **Devpod env var** (edit in infra repo): `/etc/profile.d/rune-training.sh`
   - Automatically exports `RUNE_TRAINING_DATA` in every shell session on boot
   - Uses the existing `$ARTIFACT_BUCKET` variable (no SSM call needed at runtime)
   - `$$ARTIFACT_BUCKET` → OpenTofu templatefile passes through as `$ARTIFACT_BUCKET` → bash expands to `elixirtrials-949678234935-eu-west-2-artifacts`

On the devpod, usage is just:
```bash
echo $RUNE_TRAINING_DATA
# s3://elixirtrials-949678234935-eu-west-2-artifacts/training-data/github-pairs

aws s3 ls $RUNE_TRAINING_DATA/
aws s3 sync $RUNE_TRAINING_DATA/ ./local-training-data/
```

The infra edit is in `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/infra/providers/aws/files/gpu-userdata.sh`. Want me to commit it, or do you handle infra repo commits separately?

lockfiles, generated code, binary files, and build artifacts from mined diffs to minimize token overhead in training data\n- **normalize_mined_pairs()** — Extracts per-step training pairs from mined PR trajectories (review feedback → code revision cycles), producing `{task_id, activation_text, teacher_text, metadata}` records compatible with the existing distillation pipeline\n- **Batch mining CLI** — `mine_github.py --batch` processes multiple repos from a JSON config, producing per-repo JSONL files ready for S3 upload\n- **Phase 1 repos config** — 10 curated repos across Python, TypeScript, Rust, and Go with quality filtering defaults\n\n### Training pair structure\nEach pair encodes one step for the hypernetwork:\n- **activation_text**: `## Task` + `## Current Code` (previous diff) + `## Review Feedback`\n- **teacher_text**: activation + `## Revision` (the correct code change)\n\n### New files\n- `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_diff.py` (diff compressor)\n- `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_diff.py` (9 tests, 100% coverage)\n- `libs/model-training/tests/test_mine_batch.py` (2 tests)\n- `instructions/mining_repos.json` (Phase 1: 10 repos)\n\n### Modified files\n- `libs/model-training/src/model_training/d2l_data.py` (+normalize_mined_pairs)\n- `libs/model-training/tests/test_d2l_data.py` (+9 tests)\n- `scripts/mine_github.py` (batch mode: --batch, --output-dir)\n\n### Usage\n```bash\n# Batch mine Phase 1 repos (~500 PRs, ~1500 training pairs)\nuv run python scripts/mine_github.py \\\n  --batch instructions/mining_repos.json \\\n  --output-dir data/pairs/\n\n# Upload to S3\naws s3 sync data/pairs/ $RUNE_TRAINING_DATA/\n```\n\n## Test plan\n- [x] 149 tests pass, 0 regressions (`uv run pytest libs/model-training/tests/ -v`)\n- [x] d2l_diff.py at 100% coverage, d2l_data.py at 93%\n- [x] ruff check + format clean on all files\n- [x] E2E verified: mined fastapi/fastapi PR #12101 → 5 training pairs with correct structure\n- [ ] Run full Phase 1 mining on CPU machine with GitHub token\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Create PR on rune repo"
}
